--- Page 7 ---
<meta>The text begins the preface with a traditional address to the reader. The catchword "geni-" suggests the next page will continue with "genious" or "geniuses."</meta>

# To the Reader <note>original: Ad Lectorem</note>

Reader,

We seek no *patron* <note>original: *Mecænas*, referring to Gaius Maecenas, a famous Roman protector of poets</note> to *flatter with a dedication,* nor do we crave any shelter from great *personages*; for we know that our *philosophy* is the *world's contempt*, and its *professors* are their *scorn and derision*. Therefore, we neither crave their *pardon* nor fear their *frowns*, but shall assert this truth only: that *arts have no enemies except those who are ignorant of them.* For this reason, we fear no *mob uprising* <note>original: *Jack Straw’s Insurrection*, a reference to the Peasants' Revolt of 1381; used here to mean an attack by the uneducated</note> even if it were leveled against our learning, for *true wisdom is justified by her children* <note>a biblical reference to Matthew 11:19</note>. It is to them only that we give this account of the reason for *printing this collection of these small chemical treatises*, which was (next to their usefulness) for their *preservation*, since by several in-

<summary>In this preface, the author asserts that their chemical philosophy is often mocked by the world and declares they do not need wealthy patrons, as true wisdom is only understood by the "wise." The collection of treatises was published primarily to ensure their preservation and utility.</summary>

<keywords>preface, alchemy, chemical treatises, patronage, Jack Straw, philosophy, preservation of knowledge</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface, specifically detailing the origin of the treatises in this collection and the publisher's hope to recover more lost works by the same author.</meta>

ingenious *chemists* conceived to be well worth the reading, and too good to be lost; for the smallest *treatises* on this *subject* are not always of the least worth or esteem (*for truth needs no large coverings* <note>original: "Mattle-ings," likely meaning mantlings or elaborate garments</note> *to set her forth*). And as for that small *treatise* of the *Alkahest* <note>a mythical universal solvent sought by alchemists</note>, it was bestowed upon and sent to me by a *generous stranger*. He was pleased to take notice of my care in preserving the *First Gate* <note>original: *Porta Prima*</note> at the end of *Ripley Revived* <note>a 1677 work by Eirenaeus Philalethes commenting on the 15th-century alchemist George Ripley</note>, as well as that general *invitation* in the aforementioned *book* on page 390. If any other *gentlemen* shall be pleased to *imitate* this, I hope in time we shall obtain all the lost pieces of the *works* of that *famous modern English adept* <note>a term for a master of alchemy; here referring to the anonymous author Eirenaeus Philalethes</note>, which are so greatly desired. This will be a very acceptable *service* to all *philosophers*, and not the least kindness unto *your servant*, W. C. B. <note>William Cooper, Bookseller, a well-known 17th-century publisher of chemical and alchemical texts</note>

<summary>The publisher explains that even small alchemical works are valuable and reveals that the treatise on the Alkahest was provided by an anonymous donor. He expresses a hope that other collectors will come forward with lost manuscripts by the same famous English author.</summary>

<keywords>Alkahest, George Ripley, Ripley Revived, William Cooper, English Adept, alchemy, chemical treatises, book publishing</keywords>

### T H E

# CONTENTS.

1. Eirenaeus Philalethes' Secret <note>original: "Arcanum"</note>, or Secret of the Immortal Liquor *Alkahest* <note>a mythical universal solvent sought by alchemists</note>, called *Fire-Water* <note>original: "Ignis-Aqua"</note>, in English and Latin. Page 5.
2. An Anonymous Author’s *Practice of Lights, or an Excellent and Ancient Treatise of the Philosophers' Stone.* p. 27.
3. Jan Baptista van Helmont's <note>1580–1644, a chemist and physician who bridged the gap between alchemy and modern chemistry</note> *Principle Ingredient* <note>original: "Præcipiolum," a term used by Van Helmont for a specific alchemical preparation</note>, or the Immature Mineral *Electrum* <note>an alloy of gold and silver, here referring to a specific metallic state</note>, the first metal which is the *Mineral Source* <note>original: "Minera"</note> of Mercury. p. 47.
4. Francis Anthony, M.D., his *Potable Gold* <note>original: "Aurum-Potabile," gold suspended in liquid, once believed to be a universal medicine</note>, or his Recipe <note>original: "Receit"</note>, showing his way and method how he made and prepared that most excellent medicine for the human body. p. 73.
5. Bernard, Earl of Trevisan <note>a legendary 15th-century Italian alchemist</note>, his *Treatise of the Philosophers' Stone.* p. 83.
6. Sir George Ripley’s *Bosom-book* <note>a private, portable reference book</note>, containing his Philosophical Shortcuts <note>original: "Accurta-", likely "Accurtations," meaning methods to shorten alchemical processes</note>

## The Contents.

6. — <note>continued from previous page</note> Shortcuts <note>original: "Accurtations"</note> in the making of the Philosophers' Mercury and Elixirs. p. 101.
7. Roger Bacon's <note>c. 1214–1292, a medieval English philosopher and friar often associated with early science and alchemy</note> *Mirror of Alchemy* <note>original: "Speculum Alchymiæ"</note>, *or the true Glass of Alchemy.* p. 125.
8. George Starkey’s <note>1628–1665, an influential colonial American alchemist and physician</note> *Admirable Efficacy and almost incredible Virtue of true Oil which is made of Living Sulphur* <note>original: "Sulphur-vive," referring to natural, native sulfur that has not been processed by fire</note> *using the bell method* <note>original: "per Campanam," a distillation process where vapors are collected under a glass bell-shaped vessel</note>. p. 139.
9. *Sir* Hugh Platt’s <note>1548–1608, a prolific English writer and inventor</note> *New and Artificial Remedies against Famine, written on the occasion of the great Dearth* <note>a period of extreme food scarcity or famine</note> *in the Year 1596.* p. 155.
10. *The Tomb of Semiramis* <note>original: "Tumba Semiramidis"; Semiramis was a legendary Queen of Babylon; here it refers to an alchemical allegory</note>, *Hermetically Sealed* <note>airtight; a term derived from Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical father of alchemy</note>, *which if a wise man opens, he shall find inexhaustible riches and the treasures of kings, to his satisfaction.*
*By* H. V. D.

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page begins the formal inquiry into the nature of the Alkahest through a question-and-answer format, following the title page. The author provides a foundational definition of this legendary universal solvent.</meta>

### THE
## SECRET
### OF THE
# LIQUOR
## Alkahest,

<margin>Question 1</margin>WHAT is the *Alkahest*?
**Answer.** It is a comprehensive and universal *solvent* <note>original: "Menstruum" — a substance that dissolves a solid or holds it in solution</note>. In a word, it may be called ( *Fire-Water* <note>original: "Ignis-Aqua"</note> ), a fiery water. It is a simple and immortal *essence* <note>original: "ens"</note> that is penetrative, dissolving all things into their primary liquid matter. Nothing can resist its power, for it acts without being affected by the substance it works upon <note>original: "without any reaction from the Patient"</note>. It does not suffer from anything except its equal, by which it is brought under control. After it has dissolved all other things, it remains completely unchanged in its original nature; it possesses the same potency after a thousand operations as it did during the first.

<summary>This page defines the Alkahest as a "Fire-Water" or universal solvent, an immortal essence capable of reducing all substances to their primary liquid form without losing its own strength or being altered by the process.</summary>
<keywords>Alkahest, universal solvent, Menstruum, Fire-Water, Ignis-Aqua, primary matter, alchemical theory, Eirenæus Philalethes</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page serves as the formal opening of the treatise, presenting the primary definition of the Alkahest in a catechism-like question and answer format. It continues from the title page's introduction of Eirenæus Philalethes' work on this legendary alchemical substance.</meta>

### THE SECRET
## OF THE LIQUOR
# Alkahest,

<margin>Question 1.</margin> WHAT is the **Alkahest**?
**Answer.** It is a universal and comprehensive solvent <note>original: "Universale Menstruum Catholicum"</note>. In a single word, it is "Fire-water" <note>original: "Ignis-aqua"</note>. It is a simple, immortal, and penetrating essence <note>original: "ens"</note> that dissolves all things into their original liquid matter. Nothing is able to resist its power, and it acts without any reaction from the substance being acted upon <note>original: "absque Reactione patientis"</note>. Furthermore, it is not affected by any substance except for its own equal, by which it is brought under control <note>original: "sub Jugum trahitur," literally "drawn under the yoke"</note>. After it has dissolved any other substances, it remains whole and unchanged in its original nature; it is as effective in its thousandth operation as it was in its first.

<summary>This page defines the Alkahest as a "Fire-water"—a universal solvent and immortal essence capable of reducing all matter to its primary liquid state while remaining chemically unchanged by the process.</summary>
<keywords>Alkahest, universal solvent, Fire-water, Ignis-aqua, primary matter, alchemy, Eirenæus Philalethes, Menstruum</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical catechism regarding the nature and substance of the Alkahest. It transitions from the general definition of the liquor to its specific chemical and philosophical composition as a "circulated salt."</meta>

-># The Liquor Alkahest<-

<margin>Question 3.</margin> **Of what substance is it?**
**Answer.** It is a noble circulated salt <term>A "circulated" salt is one that has been purified and transformed through a cyclical process of distillation and refluxing.</term>, prepared with wonderful art until it answers the desires of an *ingenious artist* <note>original: "Ingenious Artiſt," meaning a skilled alchemist</note>. Yet, it is not any physical <note>original: "Corporal"</note> salt made liquid by a simple solution; rather, it is a saline spirit which heat cannot solidify <note>original: "Coagulate"</note> by evaporating its moisture. It is of a spiritual, uniform substance, volatile under gentle heat and leaving nothing behind; yet this *spirit* is neither acid nor alkali, but salt.

<margin>Question 5.</margin> **Which is its equal?**
**Answer.** If you know the one, you may without difficulty know the other. Therefore, seek; for the gods have made the arts the reward of industry.

<margin>Question 7.</margin> **What is the immediate material <note>original: "next matter"</note> of the *Alkahest*?**
**Answer.** I have told you that it is a salt. The fire surrounded the salt and the water swallowed up the fire, yet did not overcome it. Thus is made the "Philosophers' Fire" of which they speak: "Common people <note>original: "The Vulgar"</note> burn with fire; we burn with water."

<margin>Question 9.</margin> **Which is the most noble salt?**
**Answer.** If you desire to learn this, descend into yourself; for you carry it about with you—both the salt and its *Vulcan* <term>Vulcan: The Roman god of fire; in alchemy, this refers to the internal heat or transformative power within a substance or the practitioner.</term>—if you are able to perceive it.

<margin>Question 11.</margin> **Which is it? Tell me, I pray you.**

<summary>This page describes the Alkahest as a "noble circulated salt" and a "saline spirit" that remains volatile and liquid under heat. It introduces the alchemical paradox of "burning with water" and hints that the source of this substance can be found within the human being.</summary>
<keywords>Alkahest, circulated salt, saline spirit, Philosophers' Fire, Vulcan, internal alchemy, Eirenæus Philalethes, universal solvent</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical catechism concerning the nature of the Alkahest, moving from its basic definition to its specific chemical and philosophical properties as a "circulated salt" and its relation to the practitioner's own nature.</meta>

-># The Secret of the Liquor Alkahest<-

3. **Question.** **Of what substance is it?**
4. **Answer.** It is a noble **circulated salt** <term>A salt that has been purified and transformed through a cyclical process of distillation and refluxing to reach a higher state of matter.</term> <note>original: "Sal Circulatus"</note>, prepared with wonderful art until it answers the prayer of the wise artist. Yet, it is by no means a physical salt made liquid by a simple re-collection <note>original: "recollatione," referring to the gathering of moisture or dissolving in water</note>; rather, it is a saline spirit which heat cannot solidify <note>original: "coagulatur"</note> by evaporating its moisture. Instead, it is of a uniform spiritual substance, volatile under light heat and leaving nothing behind. This spirit is neither acidic nor alkaline, but salty <note>original: "salsus"</note>.

5. **Question.** **Which is its counterpart?** <note>original: "Compar," referring to a matching or equal substance in alchemical pairing</note>
6. **Answer.** If you have known the like, you will not with difficulty know the counterpart; seek, for the gods have sold the arts for the price of sweat <note>original: "vendidêre Dii sudoribus Artes," a classical proverb meaning that mastery requires hard labor and effort</note>.

7. **Question.** **From what material is the *Alkahest* most immediately made?**
8. **Answer.** I have told you that it is a salt. Fire surrounded the salt and the water swallowed up the fire, yet did not overcome it. Thus is made the **Philosophical Fire** <note>original: "Ignis Philosophicus"</note> of which it is said:
> Common people burn with fire; we burn with water. <note>original: "Vulgus cremat per Ignem, nos per Aquam." This refers to the alchemical "moist heat" or solvent that can dissolve metals as effectively as fire melts them.</note>

9. **Question.** **Which is the most noble salt?**
10. **Answer.** If you desire to learn this, descend into yourself; for you carry it about with you—both the salt and its **Vulcan** <term>Vulcan: The Roman god of fire; in alchemy, this refers to the internal heat or transformative power within a substance or the practitioner.</term>—if you are able to discern it.

11. **Question.** **What is it, tell me please?** <note>original: "dic sodes"</note>

<meta>catchword: 12. R. San-</meta>

<summary>This page defines the Alkahest as a "circulated salt" that behaves like a spirit, resisting solidification by heat and acting as a "Philosophical Fire" or "burning water." It concludes with a cryptic suggestion that the key to this noble salt and its transformative fire lies within the human being.</summary>
<keywords>Alkahest, Sal Circulatus, Philosophical Fire, Vulcan, internal alchemy, salt, spirit, chemical solvent</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical discussion regarding the source of the Alkahest, shifting the focus toward the human body—specifically blood and urine—as the primary materials for extracting the "Balsam of Life" and the "Universal Salt."</meta>

-># The Secret of the Liquor Alkahest<-

12. **Answer.** Man's blood outside of the body, or man's urine—for urine is a waste product separated for the most part from the blood—each of these gives both a **volatile** <term>A substance that evaporates easily at low temperatures.</term> and a **fixed salt** <term>A substance that remains solid and stable even when heated.</term>. If you know how to collect and prepare it, you will have a most precious **Balsam of Life** <note>original: "Balsom of Life"; a substance believed to preserve health and prolong life.</note>.

13. **Question.** Is the property of **human urine** more noble than the urine of any beast?

14. **Answer.** By many degrees; for though it is a waste product only, its salt has no equal in the whole of **Universal Nature** <note>The totality of the natural world and its underlying spiritual laws.</note>.

15. **Question.** What are its parts?

16. **Answer.** A volatile part and a more fixed part; yet, according to the variety of ways it is processed, these may be altered in various ways.

17. **Question.** Are there any things in urine which are different from its innermost, specific **urine-like nature** <note>original: "specifick urinaceous Nature"</note>?

18. **Answer.** There are: namely, a **watery phlegm** <term>In alchemy, 'phlegm' refers to a tasteless, watery distillate that lacks active medicinal properties.</term> and **sea salt** (common salt) which we take in with our food. This salt remains entire and undigested in the urine and may be divided from it by separation. If salt is not used in one's food for a sufficient amount of time, this sea salt ceases to appear in the urine.

19. **Question.** Where does that **phlegm** or tasteless watery moisture come from?

20. **Answer.** It comes chiefly from our various drinks, and yet every substance has its own "phlegm."

<meta>catchword: 21. Q. Ex-</meta>

<summary>This page identifies human blood and urine as the primary sources for a "Universal Salt" and the "Balsam of Life," distinguishing the essential alchemical properties of urine from common impurities like dietary sea salt and water.</summary>
<keywords>human blood, urine, Balsam of Life, volatile salt, fixed salt, Universal Nature, watery phlegm, sea salt, alchemical separation</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical discussion from the previous page, focusing on the preparation of the "Balsam of Life" from human blood and urine. It explores the composition of urine, distinguishing between its essential salts and external elements like common sea salt and phlegm.</meta>

-># The Secret of the Liquor Alkahest<-

12. **Answer.** Human blood outside the body, or human urine—for urine is a waste product separated for the most part from the blood—each of these gives both a **volatile** <term>A substance that evaporates easily at low temperatures.</term> and a **fixed salt** <term>A substance that remains solid and stable even when heated.</term>. If you know how to collect and prepare it, a most precious **Balsam of Health** <note>original: "Balsamum salutis"; in this context, a restorative alchemical medicine.</note> will be ready for you.

13. **Question.** Is the property of human urine more noble than the urine of any beasts?

14. **Answer.** By many degrees; even though it is a waste product, its salt has no equal in the whole of **Universal Nature** <note>original: "tota universa Natura"</note>.

15. **Question.** What are its parts?

16. **Answer.** A volatile part and a more fixed part; yet, depending on the variety of ways it is processed, these are altered in many different ways.

17. **Question.** Are there any things in urine that are alien to its innermost, specific **urine-like nature** <note>original: "Natura Urinacea specifica"</note>?

18. **Answer.** There are: namely, a **watery phlegm** <term>In alchemy, 'phlegm' refers to a tasteless, watery distillate that lacks active medicinal properties.</term> and **sea salt** <note>original: "Sal marinus"; common table salt.</note> taken in with food. This salt acts as a guest and remains undigested in the urine, and it can be divided from it by **separation** <note>original: "Secretio"; the chemical process of separating components.</note>. If salt is not used in food for a sufficient amount of time, this sea salt ceases to appear in the urine.

19. **Question.** From where does that **phlegm** (or tasteless watery moisture) come?

20. **Answer.** It comes mostly from drink, and yet every substance has its own phlegm.

<summary>This page discusses the alchemical potential of human blood and urine, arguing that they contain unique salts capable of forming a "Balsam of Health." The author distinguishes between the essential "urinaceous" nature of the fluid and external additives like common sea salt and phlegm.</summary>

<keywords>Alkahest, human blood, urine, volatile salt, fixed salt, Balsam of Health, sea salt, phlegm, alchemical separation</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on the chemical composition of human urine, specifically addressing the "salt of urine" and the presence of "phlegm." -># The Liquor Alkahest<-

21. **Question.** Explain yourself more clearly?

22. **Answer.** You must know that urine is conveyed to the bladder along with what we drink, partly by the **power of separation** <note>original: "separative Virtue"; the natural ability of the body to distinguish and sort different substances.</note>. It also consists of a watery <term>tessas</term> <note>original: "Tessas"; a term used in Paracelsian medicine to describe a specific waste fluid or "excrementitious humor" of the blood.</note>. When it is separated by the scent of the **urine-like ferment** <term>An active chemical agent or "spirit" that gives urine its specific properties.</term>, it penetrates very deeply, and its saltiness remains unchanged—unless the saltiness of the blood and the urine are the same. Therefore, whatever is contained in the urine besides salt is merely useless **phlegm** <term>In alchemy, "phlegm" refers to a tasteless, watery residue that lacks medicinal or chemical power.</term>.

23. **Question.** How does it appear that there is a large amount of phlegm in urine?

24. **Answer.** It can be proven first by the taste, secondly by the weight, and thirdly by its **properties** <note>original: "Virtue".</note>.

25. **Question.** Please explain what you mean?

26. **Answer.** The salt of the urine contains everything that is truly essential to the urine. Its smell is very sharp, and its taste varies depending on how it is processed; sometimes it is salty with a specific urine-like saltiness.

27. **Question.** What have you observed regarding its weight?

28. **Answer.** I have observed this: three ounces (or slightly more) of urine taken from a healthy man will weigh about 80 grains more than the same amount of **spring water** <note>original: "Fountain-Water".</note>.

->### The Secret of the Liquor Alkahest<-

21. **Question.** Explain yourself more clearly?

22. **Answer.** Know that urine is conveyed to the bladder along with what is drunk, partly by the **power of separation** <note>original: "virtutem secreticem"; the biological or chemical ability to filter and sort substances.</note>. It also consists of a watery **teffas** <term>A term from Paracelsian medicine referring to a waste fluid or "excrementitious humor" produced by the blood.</term>. When it is separated by the scent of the **urine-like ferment** <term>In alchemy, a "ferment" is an active principle that transforms a substance into a specific form—in this case, turning waste into urine.</term>, it is thoroughly changed in its saltiness—except that the saltiness of the blood becomes the saltiness of the urine. Thus, besides the salt, everything contained in urine is merely useless **phlegm** <term>The watery, tasteless residue left over after the "essential" components have been extracted.</term>.

23. **Question.** How is it evident that a large amount of phlegm is contained in the urine <note>original: "lotio"; a Latin term for urine often used in medical and chemical contexts.</note>?

24. **Answer.** Consider it from these three points: first from the **taste**, second from the **weight**, and third from its **potency** <note>original: "virtute"; its chemical or medicinal power.</note>.

25. **Question.** What is the Mercury of this substance? <note>original: "Esto tibi Mercurius"; the question asks for the "mercurial" or volatile essential spirit of the urine.</note>

26. **Answer.** The salt of the urine contains the entire essential property of the liquid. Its smell is very sharp, and its taste varies depending on how it is processed; for example, it is sometimes salty with a specific urine-like saltiness.

27. **Question.** What have you observed regarding the weight?

28. **Answer.** This surely: that three and a fraction <note>original: "duplo plus"; literally "twice more," but in this context of specific gravity, it refers to a specific measurement over the base weight.</note> ounces of urine collected from a healthy man outweigh ordinary **spring water** <note>original: "Aqua fontana".</note> by about 80 **grains** <term>A very small unit of weight used by apothecaries and alchemists, equivalent to about 65 milligrams.</term>. 30. **Answer.** The freezing of urine by cold is proof that **phlegm** <term>In this context, the tasteless, watery part of a substance that lacks active properties.</term> is present in it. 35. **Question.** What is then left behind?

36. **Answer.** An earthy, blackish, foul-smelling residue.

38. **Answer.** It appears so to the sight, smell, and taste; yet it contains qualities that are directly contrary to each other.

## The Liquor Alkahest

...the Dulech <term>A term used in Paracelsian and Helmontian medicine to describe a stone or calculus formed in the human body, such as a kidney stone.</term> is coagulated; by the other, it is dissolved.

41. **Question.** What further?
42. **Answer.** In the coagulation of urine, its spirit of wine <note>original: "Spirit of Wine"; here referring not to alcohol from grapes, but to a volatile, spirit-like substance found within urine.</note> is discovered.
43. **Question.** Is there such a spirit in urine?
44. **Answer.** There is indeed; it truly resides in every person's urine—even that of the healthiest man—most of which may be prepared by Art <note>In this context, "Art" refers to the practice of alchemy or chemistry.</note>.
45. **Question.** How powerful is this spirit?
46. **Answer.** Its power is so great that it is to be lamented, and indeed it may move us to pity mankind.
47. **Question.** Why is that?
48. **Answer.** From this spirit, the Dulech—its most fierce enemy—has its origin.
49. **Question.** Will you give an example of this?
50. **Answer.** I will. Take urine and dissolve a suitable quantity of saltpeter <note>original: "Salt-peter"; potassium nitrate.</note> in it. Let it stand for a month, and afterwards distill it; there will come over a spirit which burns on the tongue like a coal of fire. Pour this spirit back in and cohobate <term>To repeat the distillation process by pouring the distilled liquid back onto the solid remains (the "feces") to further refine or extract properties.</term> it four or five times, drawing off no more than half the volume each time. By this process, the spirit becomes extremely piercing <note>In early chemistry, "piercing" refers to a substance's ability to penetrate other materials, often related to its volatility or solvent power.</note>, yet not in the least bit sharp. The heat which goes out in the first distillation of the liquor afterwards grows noticeably mild, and at length almost (if not altogether) vanishes; thus, the second spirit may be perceived as mild...

54. **Answer.** In a heat so low it is barely perceptible to the senses; in a vessel that is lightly closed or merely covered. It may also be sometimes hotter and sometimes cooler, provided that neither the heat nor the cold exceeds a moderate level.

55. **Question.** How may this wine-like spirit become most evident?

56. **Answer.** By a type of putrefaction which causes a ferment and triggers ebullition <term>The process of bubbling or boiling up, often as a result of fermentation.</term> (which will not happen for a long time) if the urine is kept in a wooden vessel and in a place that is not hot (but still keeps out the cold)—for example, behind a furnace in winter. ...a gentle heat for about two weeks <note>original: "the space of a fortnight or thereabout"</note> sends forth a coagulating spirit, which will coagulate well-distilled *Aqua-Vitæ* <note>original: "Aqua-Vitæ"; Latin for "water of life," referring to high-proof distilled alcohol.</note>.

59. **Question.** How is that spirit to be prepared which forms the *Duelech* <term>A term used by alchemists like Paracelsus and Van Helmont to describe a stony deposit or calculus formed in the body, such as a kidney or bladder stone.</term> by itself with a clear, watery distillation <note>original: "stalagma"; a drop or liquid produced by distillation.</note>; and also the spirit that dissolves it?

60. **Answer.** Urine fermented <note>original: "putrified"</note> for a month and a half in a heat (most similar to the heat of horse manure) will provide, in a suitable vessel, each distilled liquid <note>original: "stillatitious stalagma"</note> according to your desire.

61. **Question.** Does every spirit of urine coagulate the spirit of wine?

62. **Answer.** Not at all; this second spirit is observed to lack that power.

63. **Question.** What does urine treated this way contain besides the aforementioned spirits?

64. **Answer.** Its more fixed urine-based salt <note>original: "Urinaceous Salt"</note>, and by accident, foreign sea salt <note>original: "Marin Salt"</note>.

65. **Question.** Can this more fixed salt be brought through the alembic <term>An early chemical apparatus used for distillation, consisting of a gourd-like container and a cap (the head) with a long neck for condensing vapors.</term> with a gentle heat in the form of a liquid?

66. **Answer.** It can, but skill <note>original: "Art"</note> and ingenuity are required.

67. **Question.** Where is the phlegm <term>In early chemistry, the watery, tasteless, and odorless part of a substance produced during distillation.</term>?

68. **Answer.** In the salt; for in the preparation through fermentation <note>original: "putrefaction"</note>, the salt, being fermented in the phlegm, rises together with it.

69. **Question.** Can it be separated?

70. **Answer.** It can, but not by every practitioner.

->## The Liquor Alkahest<-

71. **Question.** What will this spirit do when it is brought to this state?
72. **Answer.** Try it, and you will marvel at what you shall see in the dissolution of bodies.
73. **Question.** Is this not the *Alkahest*? <term>The "universal solvent" sought by alchemists, believed to be capable of dissolving any substance into its primary matter without being consumed itself.</term>
74. **Answer.** This liquor cannot exist without partaking of the powers <note>original: "Virtues"</note> of human blood; and in urine, the traces <note>original: "Footsteps"</note> of those powers are observable.
75. **Question.** Does the *Alkahest* lie hidden in urine and blood, then?
76. **Answer.** Nature gives us both blood and urine; and from the nature of these, pyrotechny <term>The chemical art of working with fire and heat, a synonym for alchemy or early chemistry.</term> gives us a salt. Through our art, this salt is processed into the "circulated salt" of Paracelsus. <note>Paracelsus (1493–1541) was a famous Swiss physician and alchemist; his "circulated salt" refers to a highly purified chemical substance that has been repeatedly distilled.</note>
77. **Question.** You are being brief?
78. **Answer.** I will add this: the salt of blood must be so transformed <note>original: "transmuted"</note> by the urine-based <note>original: "Urinaceous"</note> ferment that it loses its "final life," preserves its "middle life," and retains its saltiness.
79. **Question.** What is the purpose of this?
80. **Answer.** To demonstrate the excellence found in human blood above all other blood, which is communicated to the urine (after the waste liquid <note>original: "excrementitious Liquor"</note> is separated from it). Because of this, this urine excels all others in its wonderful power.
81. **Question.** Why do you add urine?
82. **Answer.** You must know that to transform things...

<meta>This page continues the dialogue from page 20, detailing the specific alchemical relationship between human blood, urine, and the creation of the "circulated salt" used to produce the Alkahest solvent.</meta>

->## The Secret of the Liquor Alkahest<-

71. **Question.** What will this Spirit do when it has been brought to this state?

72. **Answer.** Try it, and you will marvel at what you shall see in the dissolving of bodies.

73. **Question.** Is this not the *Alkahest*? <term>The "universal solvent" sought by alchemists, believed to be capable of reducing any body into its first matter.</term>

74. **Answer.** This liquor cannot exist without the participation of the power <note>original: "virtutis"</note> of human blood; and in the urine of that same blood, its traces are observed.

75. **Question.** Does the *Alkahest*, therefore, lie hidden in urine <note>original Latin "Lotio": literally a wash, but used here for urine</note> and blood?

76. **Answer.** Nature gives us both blood and urine; and from their nature, pyrotechny <term>The alchemical art of using fire and heat to transform substances.</term> provides a salt, which our Art processes <note>original: "circulat," referring to the process of "circulation" or continuous distillation</note> into the "circulated salt" of Paracelsus. <note>Paracelsus (1493–1541) was a Swiss physician and alchemist; "circulated salt" refers to a salt purified by repeated distillations until it becomes a powerful solvent.</note>

77. **Question.** You speak in brief riddles? <note>original: "Sub brevi syrmate loqueris," literally "You speak under a short robe," an idiom meaning to speak concisely or cryptically.</note>

78. **Answer.** I will add this: the salt of blood must be so transformed <note>original: "transmutari"</note> by the urine-based ferment that it loses its "final life," preserves its "middle life," and retains its saltiness. <note>In alchemical theory, "lives" refer to different states of matter or spiritual vitality within a substance. To lose the "final life" means to be broken down from its specific form (blood) while retaining its essential properties.</note>

79. **Question.** For what purpose is this?

80. **Answer.** To communicate the dignity <note>meaning the special excellence or status</note> that exists in human blood above any other kind of blood to the urine (after the waste liquid <note>original: "excrementitio Liquore"</note> has been separated from it). Because of this, such urine is powerful with a wonderful virtue <note>original: "Virtute," meaning effective power or medicinal quality</note> beyond all others.

81. **Question.** Why do you add urine?

82. **Answer.** Ah, you should know that for the transformation of things... <note>The sentence continues on the next page.</note>

<summary>This page explores the alchemical theory that the universal solvent (Alkahest) is derived from the combination of human blood and urine, processed through "pyrotechny" into a specialized salt. It explains how the urine inherits a "dignity" or spiritual power from the blood, making it a key ingredient in transmutation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for distilling and fermenting substances to create the Alkahest, specifically focusing on the use of urine to produce a powerful salt.</meta>

# *The Liquor Alkahest*

...things, a corruptive ferment <term>A substance that causes a chemical change or fermentation, used here to break down the material.</term> is required. In this regard, all other salts are inferior to the strong salt derived from urine.

83. **Question.** Can the phlegm <term>In alchemy, "phlegm" refers to the watery, tasteless, and odorless liquid obtained during distillation, often considered the useless part of a substance.</term> be collected separately from the salt?

84. **Answer.** It can, provided the urine is not first allowed to putrefy <note>original: "putrified"; to rot or decompose to release its active components.</note>.

85. **Question.** What proportion of the liquid should be considered phlegm?

86. **Answer.** Roughly nine parts out of ten distilled from fresh urine should be discarded. The tenth part (as much as can be extracted in liquid form) is to be kept. From the dried urine remains at the bottom, use a gentle fire (which will not cause sublimation <term>The process where a solid turns directly into a gas when heated, bypassing the liquid phase.</term>) to extract the salt with water. Use an amount of water equal to half the volume of the urine from which these dregs <note>original: "Feces"; in this context, it refers to the solid sediment or residue left after distillation, not excrement.</note> were dried. Whatever is absorbed by the water should be poured off by decanting. Let it be strained or purified by deliquescence <note>original: "per deliquium"; a process where a salt absorbs enough moisture from the air to dissolve into a liquid.</note>, then filter it through a glass vessel. Pour on fresh water and repeat this process until the salt becomes pure. Finally, join this extremely foul-smelling salt with your final spirit and cohobate <term>To repeat the distillation of a liquid by pouring it back over the solid residue left in the distilling vessel to concentrate its power.</term> it.

->Praised be the name of the Lord. Amen.<-

### The Secret of the Liquor Alkahest

...is adopted as a ferment of corruption <term>A substance used to trigger a chemical breakdown or transformation.</term>, by which quality all other salts yield to the stinking salt of urine.

83. **Question.** Can the phlegm <term>In alchemy, "phlegm" refers to the watery, tasteless, and odorless liquid obtained during distillation, often considered the useless part of a substance.</term> be collected separately from the salt?

84. **Answer.** It can, provided that the urine has not putrefied <note>original: *putruerit*; rotted or decomposed</note> beforehand.

85. **Question.** What proportion of the liquid <note>original: *aquae*; literally "water," referring to the total liquid volume</note> should be judged as phlegm?

86. **Answer.** Roughly nine parts out of ten distilled from fresh urine are to be discarded; the tenth part (as much as can be extracted in the form of a liquor) should be kept. From the dried urine remaining at the bottom of the distillation vessel, extract the salt using water with a gentle heat (which does not cause sublimation <term>The process where a solid turns directly into a gas when heated, bypassing the liquid phase.</term>). Use only as much water as equals half the volume of the urine from which these dregs <note>original: *faex*; the solid residue or sediment</note> were dried. Whatever is absorbed into the water should be poured off by decanting, then strained or purified by deliquescence <term>The process of a salt absorbing enough moisture from the air to dissolve into a liquid.</term>. After this, filter the water through a glass vessel. Pour on fresh water and repeat the process until the salt is pure. Then, join that immensely foul-smelling salt with your final spirit and redistill <term>original: *cohoba*; the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid by pouring it back over the solid residue left in the vessel.</term>.

->**Blessed be the name of the Lord, Amen.**<-

### THE
# PRACTICE
### OF
# LIGHTS.

Truly, without any falsehood, the science of alchemy is real and should not be mocked or scorned, as many ignorant fools <note>original: "blind Asses"</note> do. Many men blunder around regarding this blessed tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; in alchemy, this refers to a potent refined substance or elixir capable of transforming base metals or healing the body.</note> who possess neither learning nor any insight into philosophy. Their ignorance causes great slander and mockery, bringing great shame to this noble and glorious "Science of Grace." This science and knowledge <note>original: "cunning"</note> is found only in the natural movement, or the circulation and rotation of the heavens above. Just as all natural things in this world follow their own cycles <note>original: "passing their circuit"</note>, the high Creator of Nature maintains these movements through His marvelous divine power, exactly as He established them at the beginning and in no other way.

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>The author defends alchemy as a legitimate "Science of Grace" based on the divine movements of the celestial bodies, arguing that it is only slandered because of the ignorance of unlearned practitioners.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Science of Grace, tincture, celestial movement, natural philosophy, divine creation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues from the previous defense of alchemy, transitioning from theoretical justification to practical instruction and the author's own experimental history.</meta>

( 28 )

### THE
# PRACTICE
### OF
# LIGHTS.

All the Philosophers say that no man shall achieve this Science through haste or headstrong force, but only through patience, a long duration of time, and the true regulation <note>original: "Governance"</note> of fire.

First of all, they describe this noble Science in terms of *Calcination* <term>Calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or "calx."</term>, which I have seen performed in all the ways that deceptive thinkers <note>original: "Sophisters"</note> and ignorant people describe even to this day (which is a great pity). But truly, I have found a Natural Calcination that increases the vital moisture <note>original: "moisture radical"; the essential fluid believed by alchemists to sustain the life and integrity of a substance.</note> rather than diminishing it. This <term>Calx</term> <note>original: "Calx"; the powdery residue left after a metal has been calcined.</note> is made of Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> without any other ingredient, as it is written: "nothing should be submerged in Mercury except gold" <note>original: "nihil demergeretur in Mercurio nisi solem"</note>. I kept this compound of Gold and Mercury <note>original: "compositum Solis & Mercurii"</note> in the fire night and day for an entire year. However, because of my proportions, I failed in my goal; I used a ratio of 24 parts to one and eventually abandoned that work. 

Then I tried a ratio of 3 to 1, which I kept in the fire for seven months, maintaining a heat as intense as the Sun <note>original symbol: ☉</note> in the height of Leo <note>original symbol: ♌</note> in any part of the world. My glass vessel was merely sealed with paste <note>original: "luted"</note>, which I will never trust again; hereafter, I will only trust in heat-sealing <note>original: "nipping"</note> the vessel or closing it with molten glass. At the end of the seven months (the matter being entirely melted), I opened my glass and vigorously shook the contents up and down. The substance—having been opened and melted—stuck to the top and sides of the glass as it cooled and would not flow back down.

# THE PRACTICE OF LIGHT.

And that part which descended to the bottom was black and oily <note>original: "unctuous"</note>. I let it rest in the fire that night, and in the morning, it had risen back to the top. It was *very black* and made a crackling sound <note>original: "cried"</note> as if it were being fried. So, I let it remain in the fire until the following morning, and then I found it had risen even further. I wanted to force it to descend with heat, so I built a fire on the top of the vessel. Due to the intensity of the fire—even though the <term>Lute</term> <note>a clay or cement seal used to make alchemical vessels airtight</note> was firm and strong—part of the substance leaked through the seal into the ashes. What remained in the glass was beautiful and clear, clinging to the sides of the glass. 

Then I turned the glass over, and there appeared a thousand sparks, *like very raw Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, bright and shining*—a marvelous sight. I picked these out as carefully <note>original: "subtilly"</note> as I could. Among them, I found one piece that was black on one side and as bright as pearl on the other, and another that shone with the colors of the rainbow. All the others were as bright as anything could be, so that I thought they were in their raw state <note>original: "crude"</note>.

Then I placed a portion of them in an intense fire, and they were never tarnished but remained white. I then wished to "enliven" <note>original: "quickned"</note> them with more "Living Water" <note>original: "quick Water"</note>, following the *Rules of Lully's Testament, chapter 56: "Living water is Mercury congealed into sulfur, like the solvent [menstrual] which..."* <note>original Latin: "Aqua viva est Argentum vivum congelatum in sulphur, sicut menstruale quod"</note>

Therefore, study to obtain this water, without which nothing is accomplished in this Art" <note>This entire passage is a translation of the Latin quote from Lully provided in the original text</note>.

**Chapter 86.** *This quicksilver <note>original: "Argentum vivum"</note> joined with the body are two seeds, from which we cause "living water" <note>original: "Aquam-vivam"</note> to be born: which is our quicksilver, and the "water of life" <note>original: "Aqua-vitæ"</note> which brings dead bodies back to life.*

*Quicksilver <note>mercury</note> prepared and congealed gives all the heat we need; therefore, let an oil be made from it with a corrosive <note>an acidic or caustic substance</note>, and from that let a water be drawn which dissolves all things. I have examined and tested this powder many times in this manner: I placed the silver powder into one of our **vegetable solvents** <note>original: "Menstrues"</note>, and afterward I put it into an **alembic** <note>original: "Limbeck," a distilling apparatus</note> and distilled the solvent from the powder. The powder then became like a porous **sponge** and was full of strange **colors**, and a marvelous, shining white to see. The **body** remained at the bottom, as soft as **oil** and so fine <note>original: "impalpable"</note> that no man can express its subtlety. I placed my entire **confidence** in this powder: for whatever learned men had seen this powder, they would truly have known

...understood and known, as I have proved myself by experimenting with my calces <note>original: "Calces," powders produced by heating a substance</note> in various ways. Sometimes I have worked with the *blood of common vitriol* <note>likely an oil or essence distilled from iron or copper sulfates</note>, and after I decocted <note>boiled or heated</note> it in the *vegetable mercury* <note>an alchemical solvent, often interpreted as highly rectified spirit of wine or a botanical extract</note> for ten or twelve days, I obtained fine silver from that powder in proportion to the amount used.

I have not yet concluded the secret of this, nor many other secrets of the calx, for several reasons—notably because I fear the Enemies of the World <note>those who might steal the knowledge or persecute the alchemist</note>. 

The second reason why I do not finish the work is partly due to poverty; for this science requires three specific things to reach a conclusion. One is great intelligence <note>original: "ingeny"</note> and a perfect reasoning in understanding and handling the materials. *The second is the possession of many books to lead a person from one obscure saying to another that provides enlightenment.* And the third is worldly goods to maintain the expenses and labor, specifically the substance of the two "lights" which the mercury must work upon: Gold <note>original: "*Sol*"</note> and Silver <note>original: "*Luna*"</note>. That substance shall never be lost even if the craftsman makes a mistake in his work; rather, it shall become even finer and better than it was at the beginning. Now, *alterative corruption* is always involved in generation, as the Philosopher <note>likely referring to Aristotle</note> mentions in his work *On Generation and Corruption* <note>original: "de Generatione & Corruptione"</note>, under the principle that *the corruption of one thing is the generation of another* <note>original: "Corruptio unius est Generatio alterius, &c."</note>. And if they err in this *Corruption*, yet they shall still save all the substance of the "bodies"; they shall never be the worse in terms of quantity, but rather better in quality, as is said before. Therefore, if you are a wise man, you shall keep your substance as surely as if it were in your coffer <note>a strongbox or chest for valuables</note>, for as it stands in the...

And although there were two things at first, nevertheless, when they are dissolved into clear, flowing *mercury*—and no tangible part of the *body* is seen or felt, but it passes through the *filter* as clean as *water*—then it is but one thing. And I tell you the truth: this is the very true *key* of this science. For *Merlin* <note>likely referring to the "Allegory of Merlin," a famous alchemical text</note> and many others write about this in various ways, such as in the verse of *Merlin*: *to scatter and easily extract, etc.* <note>original: "dissipare & leviter extracta"</note>. But of all the specific books I have ever read or seen, *The Star of Complexion* <note>original: "Stella Complexionis"; an alchemical treatise focused on the "complexion" or temperament of substances</note> is the father of *truth* and *doctrine*. It shows the clear light and the right way to prepare this precious *treasure*, and it explains all the *symbolic figures* of the *philosophers* openly. I have no doubt that God has the author's soul in everlasting bliss.

Blessings: For the space of thirty years, I constantly studied and busied myself with the mystery, parables, figures, and sayings of old philosophers. In this, I was marvelously blinded and mistaken <note>original: "overseen"</note>, especially by one book called the *12 Chapters* or *12 Gates in Verse in English* <note>This refers to George Ripley’s *The Compound of Alchymy* (1471), a very famous and influential alchemical poem</note>. This book was written by a capable and well-educated scholar <note>original: "sufficient Clerk"</note>, but I warn everyone to beware of him. Through him, you will never know the secret <note>original: "privity"</note>; rather, he leads you astray because he says there is only one vessel, one substance, and one furnace, and no more.

It is true that it is but one thing, as I have previously explained and shown. It is one form of a vessel, as the *Star of the Complexion* <note>original: "Stella Complexionis," a medieval alchemical treatise</note> declares: *And I say to you this one thing upon which all the wise philosophers have grounded themselves: after the full putrefaction <note>the process of rotting or breaking down matter</note> and utter rotting of the elements, they are then to be separated and each one of them well purified <note>original: "rectified"</note>, and then reduced again to the "Body" by nature in a marvelous, choice sulfur <note>original: "sulphur elect"</note>.*

And here I counsel you to practice truthfully, and to remember and consider the saying of the great philosopher *Constantine the African* <note>original: "Constantius Africanus," an 11th-century physician and translator of Arabic medical texts</note> in the *Book of Elements*. There, he shows that man is made by the entry <note>original: "ingression"</note> of contrary things, which is to be understood as the four elements. # Unripe Mineral Electrum
## (That is) the First Metal, Which is
## the Mineral of Mercury.
<note>original Latin: *Electum Minerale Immaturum (id est) Metallus primus est Minera Mercurii*</note>

Take the best quicksilver <note>original: *Argentum vivum*</note>, which you shall distill from its own mineral ore <note>original: *Minera*</note>. Use the best Hungarian ore, which contains an abundance of quicksilver; one pound of this ore will yield twelve, thirteen, or fourteen ounces of mercury. You shall distill this mineral ore (after first crushing it into a powder) in a glass retort <term>retort: a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</term> with a receiver. Then, you shall squeeze the mercury several times through leather <note>Squeezing mercury through leather or chamois was a common method to filter out solid impurities.</note>. 

If you cannot obtain any Hungarian mercury ore <note>original: *Minera Mercurii Hungarici*</note>, take Spanish mercury, which is the best quality after the Hungarian; squeeze it through leather several times. If you cannot obtain this Spanish mercury, take the best quicksilver available. You can identify the best quality this way: if you put a little of it into a silver spoon and hold the spoon over glowing coals, and when the mercury...

<summary>This page provides instructions for obtaining and purifying high-quality mercury (quicksilver) from mineral ores, specifically recommending Hungarian and Spanish sources for alchemical work.</summary>
<keywords>quicksilver, mercury, distillation, mineral ore, Hungarian mercury, Spanish mercury, glass retort, purification</keywords>

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for testing the quality of mercury (quicksilver) using a silver spoon and glowing coals, as described on the previous page.</meta>

<header>### Van-Helmont’s Works</header>

If it flies away and leaves a yellow or white spot in the spoon, this mercury will serve for the work. But if the *mercury* leaves a black spot, it is not good for any work. Take the *mercury* which leaves a yellow or white spot in the spoon and wash it ten or twelve times with salt and vinegar. Then, squeeze it through leather as many times so that it may be purged of its outward impurities <note>original: "foulness"</note>. *Hungarian* and *Spanish* mercury are naturally clean and do not need washing.

*First of all* <note>original Latin: *Imprimis*</note>, you must separate the *coagulated mercury* <note>original Latin: *Mercurium coagulatum*</note> from the *quicksilver* <note>original Latin: *Mercurius Vivus*, literally "living mercury"</note> without using any corrosive substances. The *quicksilver* shall remain "alive" (liquid) and as clear as a *Venetian mirror* <note>original: "Venice Looking-glass"</note>. This coagulated mercury is what *Paracelsus* and *Helmont* call the *precipiolum* <note>A term used by the alchemist Paracelsus to describe a specific solid precipitate of mercury.</note> or *coagulated mercury*. 

It shall be separated as a "dead" (solid) substance from its own *mineral ore* <note>original Latin: *Minera*</note>. The *quicksilver* will still be "alive" after the *precipiolum* is separated from it. The *body of the quicksilver* <note>original Latin: *Corpus Mercurii vivi*</note> is the *ore of the precipiolum*, and the *unripe mineral electrum* <note>original Latin: *Electrum Minerale immaturum*</note> is the *ore of the quicksilver*.

*Likewise* <note>original Latin: *Item*</note>, *Helmont* and *Paracelsus* mention that when the *quicksilver* <note>original Latin: *Argentum Vivum* or *Mercurius Vivus*</note> reaches its *intended destination* <note>original Latin: *loca destinata*</note> in the chemical process, it shall leave its *precipiolum* behind in the form of a coagulated mercury. The *quicksilver* itself will depart "alive" and remain as *quicksilver*.

<summary>This page describes how to test and purify mercury using salt, vinegar, and leather. It explains the alchemical theory of Paracelsus and Van Helmont regarding the separation of liquid "living" mercury from its solid "dead" precipitate.</summary>

<header>### Precipiolum 49</header>

The destined places <note>original Latin: *loca destinata*</note> where the quicksilver <note>original Latin: *argentum vivum*</note> shall leave the seed of mercury <note>original Latin: *semen Mercurii*</note> and the *precipiolum* <note>A term used by the alchemist Paracelsus to describe a specific solid substance precipitated from mercury.</note> are gold and silver. Gold is the best location because of its stability <note>original: "fixation"</note> and purity; silver is the next best.

Within this *precipiolum* lies hidden the entire art of true chemistry. If a chemist does not know how to create or separate that *precipiolum* from quicksilver <note>original Latin: *mercurius vivus*</note>, he will fail in the whole art.

This *precipiolum* is the substance from which the Philosopher’s Mercury shall be made. This occurs when it is reduced to its "first matter," which is a clear, slippery water, similar to the tears that occasionally run from our eyes. This water will "eat and drink" its own flesh and blood and multiply itself endlessly <note>original Latin: *ad infinitum*</note>. This water will also bring all gold and silver into their own "first matter." Before the *precipiolum* is brought into its first matter (that is, when it is prepared philosophically without being corrosive), it will cure triple dropsy <note>original Latin: *triplicem hydropem*; a severe accumulation of fluid in the body.</note>, gout <note>original Latin: *podagram*</note>, the venereal disease <note>original Latin: *morbum venereum*; likely referring to syphilis.</note>, and many other illnesses.

The philosophers call quicksilver <note>original Latin: *argentum vivum*</note> their "wine," and they call the *precipiolum* their "tartar" <note>In alchemy, "tartar" refers to a crystalline deposit or sediment, drawing an analogy between the refinement of mercury and the aging of wine.</note>. Together, both create the philosophers' "spirit of wine" <note>original Latin: *spiritum vini philosophicum*</note>, which they occasionally call the "Star of Mercury" <note>original Latin: *astrum Mercurii*</note>, the "Spirit of Mercury" <note>original Latin: *spiritum Mercurii*</note>, their "fire," their mineral salt <note>original Latin: *sal minerale*</note>, and salt...

...flies away <note>original: "flyes away"; referring to evaporation under heat.</note>, it leaves a yellow or white spot in the spoon. <header>### The Precipiolum</header>

The destined places <note>original Latin: *loca destinata*</note> where the quicksilver <note>original Latin: *Argentum vivum*</note> shall leave the seed of mercury <note>original Latin: *Semen Mercurii*</note> and the precipitate essence <note>original: *Præcipiolum*; a term used by Paracelsus for a solid essence derived from a liquid</note> are gold and silver. Gold is the best location because of its stability <note>original: "Fixation"</note> and purity, and silver is the next best place after gold.

Within this precipitate essence lies hidden the entire art of true <term>chemistry</term>. If a <term>chemist</term> does not know how to make or separate that precipitate from liquid mercury <note>original Latin: *Mercurius vivus*</note>, he will fail in the whole art.

This precipitate essence is the matter from which the <term>Philosophers' Mercury</term> shall be made—that is, when it is reduced to its "first matter." This takes the form of a clear water, similar to the tears that occasionally run from our eyes, and it is slippery. This water will "eat and drink" its own flesh and blood and multiply itself with it infinitely <note>original Latin: *ad infinitum*</note>. This water will also reduce all gold and silver into their own "first matter." Before the precipitate essence is brought into its first matter (provided it is prepared alchemically <note>original: "Philosophically"</note> without being corrosive), it will cure the triple dropsy <note>original Latin: *triplicem Hydropem*; a historical term for severe fluid retention/edema</note>, gout <note>original Latin: *podagram*</note>, venereal disease <note>original Latin: *morbum venerium*</note>, and many more illnesses.

The philosophers call quicksilver <note>original Latin: *Argentum vivum*</note> their "wine," and they call the precipitate essence their "tartar" <note>An analogy to the sediment found in wine barrels</note>. Both together create the philosophers' "Philosophical Spirit of Wine" <note>original Latin: *Spiritum vini Philosophicum*</note>, which they occasionally call the "Star of Mercury" <note>original Latin: *Astrum Mercurii*</note>, the "Spirit of Mercury" <note>original Latin: *Spiritum Mercurii*</note>, their "Fire," their "Mineral Salt," and "Salt..."

# Little Precipitate <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note>

...your <term>retort</term> <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances</note> and you shall find that your gold has increased significantly, if you should weigh it. Now, how shall the "Philosophical Tartar" or *Little Precipitate* be separated from our gold?

Take your gold which you have scraped out of the retort and put it into your glass mortar. Grind it into a very fine powder with your glass pestle and mix in your mercury gradually, little by little.

Your mercury will hardly mix with the gold because the gold is full of the *Little Precipitate*. Then it is time to separate the *Little Precipitate* from the gold and mercury. This is "woman's work" <note>original: *Womans work*; an alchemical term (opus mulierum) referring to a simple, gentle, or repetitive cleaning process like laundry</note>: when her clothes are dirty, she washes the filth out of them. So, I say that when your little precipitate <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note> is entirely separated from the mercury, even if you were to distill your mercury from your gold a thousand times, they will always mix harmoniously <note>original: lovingly</note> together. You will not find any powder left behind; it will all enter into the mercury, and your water will remain clear and white.

# Small Precipitate <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note>

Now, moving further with our intent to make or prepare your small precipitate <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note> for use as a medicine: pour your clear water away from the powder that lies at the bottom of the glass, so that no water remains with the powder. 

Place the glass upon some warm ashes so that the powder may be dried; the powder will look bluish-yellow. Also, put the powder into a small cucurbit glass <note>a gourd-shaped glass vessel used in distillation</note> and distill spirit of wine <note>original: *spiritus vini*; alcohol</note> from it five or six times. Then your small precipitate <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note> is ready to be used as a medicine.

->The dose is two grains, or three at the most.<-

### Preparation of the Water of Egg Whites <note>original: *Compositio Aquæ Albuminum Ovorum*</note>

Take a large quantity of eggs and boil them until they are very hard. Then, take the whites and cut them into very small pieces. Distill them using a heat bath of ashes through an alembic <note>original: *per Cineres per Alembicum*; an alembic is a type of chemical still</note> very gently until you have extracted all the water from the whites. 

Next, take the eggshells and burn them into ashes. Place these ashes in a retort <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances</note> and pour their own distilled water back over them. Distill this through a sand bath <note>original: *per arenam*</note> very strongly. Pour this water over the ashes again and distill it once more; continue this process five or six times. The water is then ready for the small precipitate <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note>—the coagulated salt of mercury.

# Van Helmont's Method

You may remember I gave you instructions that you should pour off the clear water from the *small precipitate* <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note> and dry the *small precipitate* to create a medicinal powder. Alternatively, you may reduce all metals—principally their own substance—into their "first matter" <note>the original, undifferentiated substance from which alchemists believed all metals originated</note>. This cannot be done without the salt of the small precipitate, which is hidden in the water you poured off from the *small precipitate* itself. 

Filter that same water through gray paper and set it to evaporate very gently in a round glass vessel placed in warm ashes. When the water has evaporated away, you will find a yellowish-white salt at the bottom of the glass. This is the salt of the *small precipitate* and the "key" <note>original: *Clavis*</note> of the philosophers, which they use to unlock the secrets of the small precipitates and return them to their first matter.

If you do not know this salt, you know nothing of true chemistry. This salt decreases as the moon wanes and increases during the full moon. One grain of this salt, combined with two grains of your prepared *small precipitate*, will very gently purge all gouty <term>podagrous</term>, infectious <term>venomous</term>, and fluid-heavy <term>hydropical</term> humors <note>bodily fluids believed in pre-modern medicine to regulate health; "hydropical" refers to dropsy or edema</note>.

<meta>catchword: How</meta>

<summary>This page describes how to extract a "philosopher's key"—a yellowish salt—from the runoff water of a precipitate. This salt is claimed to have both the alchemical power to dissolve metals and the medicinal power to purge harmful bodily humors.</summary>

<keywords>Van Helmont, Præcipiolum, alchemy, first matter, medicinal salt, lunar cycles, gout, dropsy, humors</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for laboratory work involving the "small precipitate" and its corresponding salt, moving from the extraction of the salt to the chemical transformation of the precipitate into a liquid "first matter."</meta>

# Præcipiolum <note>original: small precipitate</note>

### *How to return the small precipitate into its original or fluid state.*
<note>original: "first or slippery matter"</note>

Take your salt and twice as much of your small precipitate <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note>. Once the precipitate is dried, first dissolve your salt in clean, warm *water*, and pour it over the *small precipitate*. Evaporate the water away very gently in warm ashes over a low fire. Then take your *small precipitate* with its own salt and put it into a small retort <term>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</term>. Pinch the neck of the retort very tightly, or melt the glass together to seal it, or close it with *Hermes' seal* <note>an airtight, hermetic seal used in alchemy</note>. 

Then place it in a *steam bath* <note>original: *Balneum vaporosum*</note> and let it sit for six weeks to digest or decompose <term>putrefy</term>. It will turn into a slimy *water*. Then take your retort and open the neck. Place your retort in a sand furnace and cover it with an earthenware pan. Attach a receiver <term>a vessel to collect the distilled liquid</term> and seal the joints well with lute <term>a clay or paste used to make airtight seals</term>. 

First apply a slow fire, then a stronger one, and continue this until your spirits are fully condensed into *water*. At first, your spirit will appear in the form of a white cloud, and finally in the form of red clouds or red smoke. Keep the fire going until all your spirits have passed over into clear white *water*. When you see this sign, remove the receiver from the retort and seal the receiver very well with wax so that no spirit can escape. Then let your fire go out, then take...

<page-num>61</page-num>
<header>The Small Precipitate <note>original: *Præcipiolum*</note></header>

Keep the material in a bolt-head <term>a round-bottomed glass flask with a long neck</term> and place it very deep in a sand cupel <term>a heat-resistant container used here as a sand bath</term>. Give it a very strong fire for twenty-four hours, then let the fire go out in the cupel. Stir the powder with a wooden stick and pour upon it half of your clean mercurial water. Cork the glass or seal the opening by melting the glass; then shake it and let it stand in a warm place for three or four days to undergo digestion <term>a process of slow, steady heating to encourage chemical reaction</term>. 

Pour this liquid off into another glass vessel and pour the remaining half of the water onto the leftover powder. Seal the mouth of the glass again and let it stand for another three or four days in a warm place for digestion. After that, pour it out into the previous water and seal the glass or melt the opening very tightly so that no spirit <note>in alchemy, "spirit" refers to a volatile substance or a liquid produced by distillation</note> can escape. 

Let it stand in your steam bath <note>original: *Balneo Vaporoso*</note> for eight days, and then distill it through a retort <term>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</term>. If any material remains in the retort—which will be very little—pour your spirit onto it again and distill it until everything has passed through.

Now the salt is mixed with its own spirit and brought into the first matter <note>original: *prima materia*; in alchemical theory, this is the primitive, formless base of all matter</note>. Keep it very well sealed.

<page-num>62</page-num>
<header>Van Helmont's</header>

This is the "Water" to which the philosophers <note>The "philosophers" usually refers to alchemists or natural philosophers</note> have given many—indeed, more than a hundred—names, such as their "horse-dung" <note>A source of gentle, steady heat in alchemy</note>, their "Bath of Mary" <note>original: *Balneum Mariæ*; a water bath or double boiler</note>, their "quicklime" <note>original: *Calx Vive*</note>, and, in short, this is the philosophers' true fire. Without this fire or water, no man can perform any true work in chemistry <note>original: *Chimitry*</note>.

Once the philosophers have produced this "salamander" <note>In alchemy, the salamander represents a substance that can endure fire without being consumed</note>—which will never waste away in the fire, but rather grows stronger the longer it remains there—this water will increase or multiply infinitely <note>original: *per Infinitum*</note>. That is to say, if the entire sea were mercury, this water would transform it all into the first matter <term>the primitive, formless base of all matter in alchemical theory</term>.

First, you must wash your mercury with salt and vinegar several times, and finally with water to wash away the salt. Then, mix this mercury with quicklime and calcined tartar <term>potassium carbonate produced by heating cream of tartar</term>. Distill it in a retort <term>a glass vessel with a downward-pointing neck</term> within a sand furnace, and attach a receiver <term>a vessel to collect the distillate</term> filled almost half-full with water. When your mercury is dry, squeeze it eight or ten times through leather; then your mercury is well-purified of its impurities.

This is the mercury you shall use in multiplying your spirit or the "Star of Mercury" <note>original: *Astrum Mercurii*; likely referring to a purified or celestial essence of mercury</note>.

Take one part of this purified <note>original: purged</note> mercury and two parts of your spirit or Star of Mercury <note>original: *Astrum Mercurii*</note>. Put them together, mixed, into a bolt-head <term>a glass flask with a long neck and a round body</term>. Seal the bolt-head well and let it stand in a warm place for one night; your mercury will melt into your spirit or Star of Mercury and turn it into water. Then, distill it through a retort <term>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances</term>. You may repeat this process by adding your water to fresh mercury as many times as you please. This "water" will break down or dissolve all stones, gold, and silver into their first matter <term>the primitive, formless base of all matter in alchemical theory</term>. Also <note>original: *Item*</note>, it will carry gold and silver over with it through a retort, and that gold and silver will never be separated from one another.

The reason for this is that gold, silver, and all metals are of the same nature and have their beginning from the same "Water"; there is nothing in the world that does not have its beginning from it.

### *The Oil of Gold*

Take one part gold and five or six parts of the best mercury. Mix them both together <note>forming an amalgam</note>, and then distill the mercury away from the gold. This is (says the author) one of the greatest medicines under the sun, and you can hardly find a better.

Three drops are able to extinguish any sickness, and in this <term>Oil of Gold</term> <note>original: *Oyl of Gold*</note> is the greatest secret of nature.

With this liquor, *Raymond Lully* describes <note>original: *figureth*</note> mercury in *Nature Disemboweled* <note>original: *Exenterata Natura*, an alchemical work attributed to Lully</note>.

These processes were given by a *German* to Mr. *Richard Pratt*, who lived for some time in *Hampshire* and cured the *German* of a sickness. In exchange for this kindness, the *German* gave him these processes, protesting that they were some of *Van Helmont's* <note>referring to Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a famous Flemish chemist and physician</note>. Mr. *Pratt* then gave them to Mr. *Yalden*, the minister of *Compton* near *Godalming* <note>original: *Godlyman*</note> in *Surrey*, who sent them to Dr. *Salmon* to print. Since he neglected to do so for some years, they were sent—along with Dr. *Anthony's* recipe for his <term>drinkable gold</term> <note>original: *Aurum potabile*</note>—to *William Cooper* to print for the benefit of the public. Since I received them, I find Dr. *Salmon* has newly printed them in his *Medical Gift* <note>original: *Doron Medicum*</note> called <term>Golden Liquor</term> <note>original: *Liquor Aureus*</note>, Volume 2, Book 11, Section 2, pages 520 to 523. As for the <term>Immature Mineral Electrum</term> <note>original: *Electrum minerale Immaturum*</note> which he calls *Idea*, see the *Medical Gift* <note>original: *Doron*</note> Volume 1, Chapter 27, pages 315 to 326. However, it is very much mangled and altered by his use of fake names, when in fact he received—

## POTABLE GOLD: <note>original: "AURUM-POTABILE," Latin for "drinkable gold."</note>

->OR THE<-

# RECIPE <note>original: "RECEIT"</note>

->OF<-

### Dr. FRANCIS ANTHONY. <note>original: "FR. ANTONIE." Francis Anthony (1550–1623) was a famous English goldsmith and physician known for promoting his "panacea" made from gold.</note>

->SHOWING,<-

->His way and method for how he made and prepared that most excellent medicine for the human body.<-

---
