There is another way to **calcine** gold so that it may be reduced into the "First Matter" or the "Mercury of the Philosophers." **Paracelsus** <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist who pioneered the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine.</note> declares this in his seventh book of *Metamorphosis* concerning resuscitation: that the metal must be **calcined** with revivified **mercury**. This is done by putting **mercury** with the metal into a **sublimatory** <term>sublimatory: a vessel used for the process of sublimation</term> and digesting them together until an *amalgam*...

## CHAPTER IV.

### Regarding the second and true *Philosophical Solution of Bodies*, and their *reduction into Mercury*.

Having performed **calcination** (or the "first solution") which we described in the previous chapter—and which, as the anonymous philosopher states in his response in the *Golden Treatise of the Philosophers' Stone*, ought to be gentle and completely natural—the process should dissolve the **subject** <term>subject: the primary material being worked upon, such as a metal</term> without violence <note>original: "without noise"</note> while preserving its **radical moisture** <term>radical moisture: the fundamental liquid essence that alchemists believed was the source of life and growth within a substance</term>. After this, the calcined bodies must be placed into a **hermetically** <term>hermetically: sealed airtight, usually by melting the glass neck of a flask to ensure no vapors escape</term> [sealed phial...]

...closely sealed, and in the gentle heat of a **water bath** <note>original: *Bal. Mar.* or *Balneum Mariae*; a vessel of hot water used to heat another container</note> or "Dew," let it be digested or **putrefied** <term>putrefied: in alchemy, the process of breaking down a substance through gentle, moist heat to prepare it for transformation</term> for the duration of a **Philosophical Month** <note>a symbolic period of time in alchemy, often forty days, representing a full cycle of transformation</note>. For a natural dissolution is better than a violent one, and a moderate one better than a fast one, as the Philosopher says. 

And in this way, the second and true **solution** <term>solution: the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid</term> of a metal into "viscous water" is achieved—or into a certain **oiliness** <note>original: "Oleity"</note>—while preserving the **radical moisture** <term>radical moisture: the fundamental, life-sustaining fluid that alchemists believed existed within all matter</term>. Within this liquid is the true metallic **Sulphur**, together with the true and most noble **Mercury**. One of them always acts as the **magnet**, remaining in the solution with the dissolved material; it desires to stay inseparable because of the similarity of their substances. 

This is why the Ancients said, *"Nature rejoices in Nature; Nature overcomes and alters Nature."* <note>original: *Natura natura laetatur, natura naturam vincit, natura naturam admittat*; a foundational maxim of alchemy</note>. By this principle, the "essential" or "formal" solution is distinguished from a "corrosive" solution (which would destroy the metal's essence). 

But you must know that from **Silver** <note>original: *Luna*</note> a liquid or green tincture is obtained, which is the true **Elixir of Silver** and the highest **secret remedy** <note>original: *Arcanum*</note> for comforting the brain. However, from **Gold** <note>original: *Sol*</note>, through the same process of putrefaction, a liquid of the deepest redness is produced; this is the true **Elixir of Gold** and the **quintessence** <term>quintessence: the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance, believed to be its fifth element</term> of the metal. 

Regarding this, **Geber** <note>Geber is the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, an 8th-century polymath and a foundational figure in alchemy</note> says: *"We create a blood-red gold better than any produced by Nature—a form of gold that Nature itself does not make."* Concerning this **viscosity** <note>thickness or stickiness</note>, Geber further explains briefly: *"We have most exactly..."*

# The Tomb of Semiramis <note>original: *Tumba Semiramidis*</note>

...tested all things, and that by proven reasoning, but we could never find anything permanent in **fire**, except the **viscous moisture**, the sole root <note>original: *radix*</note> of all **metals**. While all other **moistures**—not being well united in a state of homogeneity <term>homogeneity: a state where all parts are of the same kind; uniform and inseparable</term>—easily flee from **fire**, and the **elements** are easily separated from one another, the **viscous moisture** (namely, **Mercury**) is never consumed by **fire**. Nor is the **water** separated from the **earth**; instead, they either remain together entirely, or they depart together entirely.

But you may ask: in what weight or proportion should the **solvent** <term>Menstruum: an alchemical solvent used to dissolve solid materials</term> be joined <note>original: *espoused*</note> to a **metal**? The ***Philosophers' Rosary*** <note>original: *Rosarium Philosophorum*; a famous 16th-century alchemical treatise</note> says,

> Just as in the making of bread, a little **leaven** <note>leaven: a substance like yeast that causes dough to ferment and rise</note> leavens and ferments a great quantity of dough; so also a small amount of **earth** is sufficient for the nourishment of the whole **Stone**.

**Aristotle** <note>likely referring to pseudo-Aristotelian alchemical texts popular in the Early Modern period</note> specifies the weight, saying,

> Proceed in this way, and cook <term>coct: from the Latin *coquere*, meaning to boil, ripen, or process through heat</term> until the **earth** (that is, the **gold**) has exhausted ten parts of the **water**.

The author of the ***New Light*** <note>original: *Novum Lumen Chymicum*; an influential alchemical work by Michael Sendivogius</note> at the end of his **book** breaks out into these words:

> There ought to be ten parts of **water** to one part of **body**. By this method, we make **Mercury** without using common mercury, by taking ten parts of our **mercurial water** (that is, the **mercurial oil of salt**, putrefied and distilled <note>original: *alembicated*; processed through an alembic or still</note>), which is an oily vapor, for every one part of the body of **gold**. When these are enclosed in a vessel through continual **cooking**, the **gold** is made...

## CHAPTER V.

### What a Quintessence properly is.

**Paracelsus** <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a Swiss physician and alchemist</note> in his third book *Of Long Life*, chapter 2, discourses thus: 

> A **Quintessence** <term>Quintessence: literally the "fifth essence" or "fifth element"; in alchemy, it is the purest and most concentrated essence of a substance, believed to have medicinal or transformative powers</term> is nothing else but the goodness of Nature, so that all of Nature passes into a spagyric <term>spagyric: the alchemical process of separating a substance into its components and then recombining them in a purified form</term> mixture and temperament <term>temperament: a balanced combination of qualities or elements</term>, in which no corruptible thing and nothing contrary <note>meaning opposing elements that cause decay</note> is to be found.

In his fourth book, *The Archidoxes* <note>original: *Archidox.*; a foundational work by Paracelsus on the mysteries of nature</note>, regarding the Quintessence, he also says:

> A **Quintessence** is a matter which is physically <note>original: *corporally*</note> extracted out of all growing things <note>original: *Crescitives*; from the Latin *crescere*, referring to anything that grows, such as plants or minerals</note>, and out of all things that have life, being separated from all impurity and mortality, most purely refined <note>original: *subtiliated*</note>, and divided from all its elements.

And a little after in the same place:

> You ought to know concerning the **Quintessence** that it is a very small amount of matter, lodged and harbored within some tree, herb, stone, or the like; the rest is a physical body <note>original: *pure body*; here meaning the bulk or mass of the material</note>, from which we learn the separation of the elements.

**Rupescissa** <note>Johannes de Rupescissa (c. 1310–1362), a Franciscan friar and influential alchemist</note> concerning the Quintessence, in chapter 5 near the end, says, 

> The **Quint-**

# Chapter V.
## What a Quintessence properly is.

Paracelsus <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a pioneering Swiss physician and alchemist</note>, in the second chapter of his third book *On Long Life* <note>original: *De Vita Longa*</note>, argues this way: *A quintessence is nothing else but the goodness of nature, such that all of nature passes into a spagyric <term>spagyric: a method of herbal medicine involving the separation and recombination of plant components; an alchemical preparation</term> mixture and temperament <note>a balanced state of qualities</note>, in which no corruptible thing and nothing opposing <note>original: *contrary*</note> is to be found.*

In the fourth book of his *Archidoxes* <note>original: *Archidoxis Magicae*, one of Paracelsus's most influential alchemical works</note> concerning the quintessence, he also says: *A quintessence is a substance <note>original: *matter*</note> which is physically extracted out of all growing things <note>original: *crescitives*: things that grow, like plants or animals</note>, and out of everything that has life, having been separated from all impurity and mortality, most purely refined <note>original: *subtiliated*</note>, and divided from all its constituent elements.*

And a little later in the same section: *Regarding the quintessence, you should know that it is a very small amount of matter <note>original: *a matter little and small*</note> lodged and harbored within some tree, herb, stone, or similar thing; the remainder is a pure body <note>the physical husk or matrix</note> from which we learn how the elements are separated.*

Rupescissa <note>Johannes de Rupescissa (Jean de Roquetaillade), a 14th-century Franciscan friar famous for his work on the "essence" of wine or alcohol</note>, writing about the quintessence in chapter five, says near the end: *The Quint—*

-> The Tomb of Semiramis <note>original: *Tumba Semiramidis*</note> <-

...essence which we seek is therefore a thing created <note>original: *ingeniated*</note> by divine breath, which by continual ascensions and descensions <note>the process of evaporation and condensation in an alchemical vessel</note> is separated from the corruptible body of the *four elements* <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</note>. The reason for this is that whatever is sublimated <note>purified by being turned into vapor and then back into a liquid or solid</note> a second time, or many times over, is more *refined* <note>original: *subtile*</note>, *exalted* <note>original: *glorified*</note>, and *separated* from the *corruption* of the *four elements* than when it ascends only once. 

And so, that which is sublimated even up to a *thousand times*, and through continual ascension and descension reaches such a great *power* <note>original: *vertue*</note> of *exaltation* <note>original: *glorification*</note>, becomes a *compound* that is almost incorruptible, like the *heavens* and the *matter of the heavens*. Therefore, it is called the *Quintessence* <note>literally the "fifth essence," a celestial substance distinct from the four earthly elements</note> because it relates to the *body* in the same way the *heavens* relate to the *whole world*. In this way, *Art* <note>the practice of alchemy</note> can imitate *Nature* through a similar, very close, and *natural* <note>original: *connatural*</note> process.

### The Tomb of Semiramis <note>original: *Tumba Semiramidis*</note>

Riches! Oh, the Water of our Sea! Oh, our saltpeter <note>original: *Sal Nitre*</note> belonging to the Sea of the World! Oh, our Vegetable! Oh, our fixed and volatile Sulphur! Oh, the dead head <note>original Latin: *Caput mortuum*; refers to the useless residue left after distillation</note>, or the dregs <note>original: *fæces*</note> of our Sea!

Tridensine <note>possibly a reference to an author or text from the Council of Trent era, or a pseudonym</note>, in his secret work on the Philosopher’s Stone, says: 

> The Water which philosophers used for the completion <note>original: *complement*</note> of the Work, they called Virgin’s Milk <note>original Latin: *Lac Virginis*</note>, the Curd <note>original Latin: *Coagulum*</note>, the Morning-Dew, the Quintessence, Water of Life <note>original Latin: *Aqua-vitæ*</note>, the Philosopher’s Daughter, and so on.

Paracelsus <note>a famous 16th-century Swiss physician and alchemist</note> also named it variously: *Azoth* <note>a term for the universal solvent or the essential agent of transformation</note>, Spirit of Wine tempered and circulated, or Mercurial-Water. Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius, a prominent Polish alchemist</note> called it Steel <note>original Latin: *Chalibs*</note>; Rupescissa <note>Johannes de Rupescissa, a 14th-century Franciscan alchemist</note> called it vinegar most nobly distilled.

Van Helmont <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont, a 17th-century chemist and physician who bridged the gap between alchemy and modern science</note> (that most profound philosopher by fire) called it the liquid *Alkahest* <note>a hypothetical universal solvent capable of dissolving any substance</note> and described it thus: 

> The liquid *Alkahest* dissolves <note>original: *resolves*</note> every visible and tangible body into its primary matter, while preserving the power of the "Seed" <note>the generative essence within matter</note>. Regarding this, the chemists say that the common people <note>original: *Vulgar*</note> burn things by fire, but we burn them by water.

We, with the philosophers' permission, are among those who can name their products at will; we call it the Mercurial Oil of Salt, putrefied and distilled <note>original: *alembicated*; referring to the use of an alembic still</note>. For oil is raised <note>original: *exalted*</note> to a higher degree of a fiery quality, as it is the foundation of the entire metallic solution (which is something to be carefully noted).

# The Tomb of Semiramis <note>original Latin: *Tumba Semiramidis*</note>

...without which nothing can be successful <note>original: "advantagious"</note> in the Alchemical Art. It plays the role of the Woman in our Work and is deservedly called the Wife of Gold <note>original Latin: *Sol*</note> and the Womb <note>original: *Matrix*</note>. It is the hidden Key to opening the locked gates of Metals; for it dissolves metals reduced to powder by heat <term>calcined</term>, and it burns to ash and decomposes <note>original: "putrefies"</note> things that are volatile and spiritual. It stains <note>original: "tingeth"</note> substances with every color and is the beginning, middle, and end of the alchemical infusions <term>tinctures</term>. It is of the same nature as Gold, as Arnaldus de Villa Nova <note>a famous 13th-century physician and alchemist</note> confirms, except that the nature of Gold is complete, refined <note>original: "digested"</note>, and stable <term>fixed</term>, whereas the nature of this Water is incomplete, unrefined, and easily evaporated <term>volatile</term>. In short, it is the Philosophers’ Fire, by which the Tree of Hermes <note>an alchemical symbol for the process of growth and transformation within the flask</note> is burned to ashes.

Regarding this Fire, Johannes Pontanus <note>an alchemist whose famous "Epistle" describes the secret fire necessary for the work</note> says in his Letter:

> The Philosophers’ Fire is not the fire of a water bath <note>original Latin: *Balneo*</note>, nor of horse dung <note>both common sources of gentle heat in early chemistry</note>, nor any such thing that the philosophers have published in their writings. It is mineral-based; it is steady and continuous; it does not evaporate unless it is overheated <note>original: "incensed"</note>. It shares the nature of Sulphur and is obtained from a source other than the primary material itself. It divides, dissolves, burns to ash, and solidifies <note>original: "congeals"</note> all things. It is a fire that burns moderately and is a shortcut <term>compendium</term> that requires little expense, because the entire work is completed with it. Study it deeply, therefore: for if I had...

I have said enough.

GEBER, in *The Sum of Perfection* <note>original Latin: *Summa perfectionis*</note>, Chapter 52, seems to be of this opinion when he says, *Everything that is dissolved must necessarily have the nature of salts, alums, and the like.* And *Paracelsus* <note>a famous 16th-century Swiss physician and alchemist</note>, in his fourth book of *Archidoxes* <note>a work on the "primary teaching" or mysteries of nature</note> concerning the quintessence <term>the purest essence of a substance</term>, shortly after the beginning, says this: *It is difficult, and scarcely credible, to extract a quintessence without a corrosive out of metals, but especially out of gold, which cannot be overcome except by a corrosive, by which the...*

# The Tomb of Semiramis <note>original Latin: *Tumba Semiramidis*</note>

...quintessence and body are one separated from the other; from which the corrosive may again be removed. And in Chapter 3 of *On Long Life* <note>original Latin: *De Vita Longa*</note>, Volume 6, Book 3, he speaks thus: *Dissolve gold together with all the substance of gold by a corrosive, etc., and do so until it becomes the same as the corrosive. Do not be dismayed by this method of operation; for a corrosive is beneficial <note>original: "commodious"</note> for gold, if it be truly gold, and without a corrosive it is dead.*

Yet you must know that our solvent <term>menstruum</term>, being poured upon gold, should not properly be called corrosive, but rather fiery; the strength and power <note>original: "vertue"</note> of this secret <term>arcanum</term> overcomes all poisons. For every arsenic-like mineral <term>realgar</term>—that is, quicksilver <note>original: "Mercury vive"</note> and its chemically altered forms <term>sublimate</term> and <term>precipitate</term>—must "die" in the Elixir of the Sun <note>original Latin: *Elixirium of Sol*, referring to gold</note> and transform into a unique and excellent medicinal color <term>tincture</term>. This is because the violent dissolution caused by other substances—such as the restoratives <term>resuscitatives</term> known as *Aqua Fortis* <note>nitric acid</note> and *Aqua Regia* <note>a mixture of nitric and hydrochloric acids capable of dissolving gold</note>, and others of that kind—is not caused by our solvent. Instead (as was said before in the fourth chapter), the dissolution happens gently and quietly, without any noise, and while preserving the substance's essential internal fluid <term>radical moisture</term>. As Lully <note>Ramon Llull, a 13th-century philosopher whose name was often attached to later alchemical texts</note> writes in his handbook <note>original Latin: *Vade Mecum*, meaning "Go with me"</note>, a life-giving power is infused into the materials through these spirits.

## CHAPTER VIII.

### *On the Practice of the Stone.*

When you have acquired the coloring <term>tinging</term> Soul of the Planet <note>in alchemy, planets correspond to specific metals; for example, Sol is Gold</note>, or its true essence <term>Quintessence</term> through the previous process of breakdown <term>putrefaction</term>—in which the true Mercury and the Philosophers' true Sulfur are contained—then your matter is prepared and fit to be made into our blessed Stone. 

Therefore, take (in the name of Him who spoke and all things were created) a sufficient quantity of this most pure matter and put it into a stabilizing <term>fixing</term> vessel, a phial, or a "Philosophical Egg" <note>a round, egg-shaped glass flask used for long-term heating</note>, hermetically sealed. Place it in a furnace <term>Athanor</term>, as you know, and proceed with a suitable, constant heat for digestion. This heat must be continual (for if it fails, the work will surely die or be miscarried <note>original: "become abortive"</note>), gentle <note>original: "sweet"</note>, subtle, transformative <note>original: "altering"</note>, and not burning—to use the Count's <note>likely referring to the Count of Trevisan, a famous 15th-century alchemist</note> own words—from the first joining <term>conjunction</term> until the final purification <term>ablution</term>. 

Regarding the management of the fire, the anonymous author Philalethes <note>Eirenaeus Philalethes, a highly influential but anonymous alchemist of the 17th century</note> may be further consulted; he clearly describes the various changes of colors according to the influence of every planet, as the matter thickens <term>coagulating</term> and stabilizes <term>fixing</term> into the white or 

<meta>catchword: red</meta>

## CHAPTER X.

### *Concerning the medical <term>physical</term> use of the Stone, both internal and external.*

You must know regarding this blessed Stone that it is a universal medicine, containing within it the perfect cure for all diseases—both hot and cold—so far as they are known to be curable by nature and are permitted by God to be cured. If you ask how this most perfect medicine, this "Celestial Tincture," and other such universal cures act and operate by curing opposite conditions in the human body, we answer: they perform all this by heating, illuminating, and radiating the Archeus <term>the vital life force or "internal chemist" believed to govern the body's health</term>, as our philosopher Van Helmont <note>Jan Baptist van Helmont (1580–1644), a Flemish physician and chemist</note> has it in a treatise entitled *In Words, Herbs, and Stones there is Great Power* <note>original: *In verbis, herbis, et lapidibus est magna virtus*</note>. <meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Philosopher's Stone acts as a universal medicine by influencing the body's internal life force (the Archeus), providing specific dosages and instructions for both internal and external healing.</meta>

-># The Tomb of Semiramis <note>original Latin: *Tumba Semiramidis*</note> 32<-

...netration, entry <term>intro-admission</term>, mixing <term>commixture</term>, and exchange <term>commutation</term>. They also act from a distance upon the drowsy or inflamed Archeus <term>the vital life force or "internal chemist" believed to govern the body's health</term>, as if by sight alone. This occurs through the radiation or emission <term>ejaculation</term> of their powers <term>virtues</term> which are produced and displayed, while their original weight and properties are still kept and not changed. In this same way, as Joachim Poleman <note>Joachim Poleman was a 17th-century chemist and follower of Paracelsus and Van Helmont</note> excellently says: *They transform the spirits of darkness—that is, diseases (which are nothing other than the characteristics of the realm of death, or the forerunners of dark and obscure death)—into good spirits, like those the person had when in perfect health. The dose is one to two grains, depending on the age and strength of the patient, taken in a drink of warm wine or in a spoonful of the same dissolved Quintessence <term>the purest essence of a substance</term> every third day. For external diseases—such as wounds, malignant <term>cacoetheck</term> and spreading <term>phagedenic</term> ulcers, fistulas, gangrene, cancer, and so on—one grain is taken in wine every day or every other day. Additionally, the externally affected part should be washed in wine in which a portion of our Stone has been dissolved. If necessary, it may be injected with a syringe, followed by applying a lead plate and a suitable bandage <term>ligature</term>.

And this is the completed internal and external use of this great Mystery. To acquire it, call upon the Light of Light <note>a reference to God or divine wisdom, from the Latin *Lumen de Lumine*</note> and pray with a pure heart for the enlightenment of your understanding, and you shall receive it. Then, work carefully, help the poor, do not misuse God's blessings, believe the Gospel, and practice piety. 

*Amen.*

<meta>This page continues George Starkey's explanation of the medical "Pill" often attributed to Richard Matthews. Starkey clarifies that the formula was originally intended to be a versatile base that could be adapted for many different treatments, rather than a single "universal" cure.</meta>

...I viewed it as a storehouse or common resource <note>original: Common place</note> from which various medical compositions might arise. For the "Corrector" (as Mr. Matthews calls it) is a method by which all plant-based harmfulness <note>original: vegetall malignity</note> is totally eliminated. It is a true, natural way to prepare solid vegetable extracts <term>original: concretes; solid forms of substances produced by chemical processes</term>. These extracts, being made of alkaline salts <note>original: salts alcalizate</note> and essential oils joined together, could be enhanced with oil of cinnamon, mace, cloves, etc., or any other kind as desired. 

They could be further enriched with any spirit or tincture. In addition to the changes made by using various solid extracts, the scope <note>original: latitude</note> of that Pill—as the need arises—extends to include every herb, seed, root, gum, and flower in the world. All oils and salts (both volatile and fixed) derived from herbs, as well as all spirits and tinctures, may also be used at the proper time and place to alter and vary this preparation as the occasion requires.

Now, since Mr. Matthews was not a scholar and did not dare to claim he could give medical advice as a physician, he was forced to take this approach: he chose one more general composition whose range of effectiveness <note>original: latitude of virtue</note> was broader than other specific formulas (where opium is either completely omitted or added in small amounts) and promoted it as a universal remedy. He did this because if he had suggested that the Pill could be varied according to need, he would have been forced to use his own judgment in advising when, where, and how to make those changes—which he...

Anyone who has read or shall read my published books—namely, *Nature’s Explication*, *Helmont’s Vindication*, and *Pyrotechny Asserted* <note>original: "Pyrotechny Asserted"; a defense of "fire-technology," meaning the chemical or alchemical art of preparing medicines</note>—will find the foundation of these and far more noble preparations. They will see that these pills and other specific remedies are only substitutes <term>original: succedaneous; a medicine that may be substituted for another</term> for even greater secrets. Yet, they are sufficient for the full cure of many diseases that require nothing else; and where the desired effect can be achieved by a lesser remedy, why should a rarer and more secret one be sought? I will therefore say no more by way of introduction but get straight to the business at hand. I will explain the virtues and the manner of use for several medicines made and offered for sale by me. And first:

### *My Sweat-Inducing, Urine-Promoting, and Pain-Relieving Elixir.* 
<note>original: Diaphoretic, Diuretic, and Anodynous Elixir</note>

Its operation works through sweat and urine, easing pains or any common violent disturbance in the body. It calms the *Archeus* <term>original: Archeus; in Helmontian medicine, the internal life-force or "spirit" governing the body’s functions</term> and, through its ability to dissolve blockages <note>original: resolutive faculty</note>, it opens obstructions very powerfully and pleasantly. It is a medicine that deserves great praise and has been very famous throughout all England and in neighboring countries under Mr. Matthews's name.

This version of mine surpasses his beyond comparison, as I will appeal to any man's experience to prove. For his version was based on the method I used in my younger [years...]

In this way, nature finds help gradually <note>original: insensibly</note> and often recovers beyond expectations.

The ordinary dose for a man is 20 to 24 **grains** <term>a small unit of pharmaceutical weight, roughly 65 milligrams</term>, sometimes up to 30; but in fevers, 20 grains is as large a dose as is necessary. It should be repeated once every 12, 16, or 20 hours, depending on how violent the disease is. The same dose and observation apply to **pleurisy** <note>original: Plurisies; inflammation of the lung lining</note>, **heatstroke** <note>original: Calentures; fevers often caused by tropical heat</note>, **measles**, **purples** <term>a term for fevers with purple spots on the skin, often referring to typhus or similar infections</term>, or any acute disease that most severely threatens life.

Where the person is young or weak, 8 or 10 grains is a dose; for very young children, 3 or 4 grains are sufficient and may be repeated as the situation requires until recovery. With God's blessing, this will occur in three or four days, and sometimes in just one.

...dose, or two at most, the patient <note>original: party</note> recovers so well as to need no more.

In **coughs** and **fluxes** <term>fluxes: a historical term for dysentery or excessive discharge from the bowels</term>, it is an excellent remedy. It should be taken at bedtime <note>original: bed-ward</note>, either dissolved or as a **pill**, followed by a generous drink of warm wine. The dose for these conditions is the same as for fevers, only given at bedtime. However, if the disease is so severe that the patient is confined to bed, give the medicine at any time. The patient should refrain from eating for an hour or two after taking it and repeat the dose more often until recovery.

Where the patient is still able to walk about, take it only in the evening and lie down in a way that encourages sweating. Repeat this every night for three or four nights in a row, then stop for two or three days. During those days, take six or eight grains of the true **Essence of Venus** <note>original: Ens veneris; a medicinal preparation usually made from copper or iron salts and sal ammoniac, believed to act as a powerful tonic</note> every morning and evening. After that, take this pill again for three or four evenings, then the Essence of Venus for two or three days. Continue alternating both treatments until perfect health is restored, which, with God’s blessing, will be in a short time.

In **agues** <term>agues: a fever marked by regular fits of shivering and sweating, often associated with malaria</term>, give 20, 24, or 30 grains of this pill in a single dose about two hours before the fever fit is expected. It may be taken either dissolved or as a pill. Follow it with a large drink of warm ale or **mulled wine** <note>original: burnt Wine</note>, then lie down and stay warm. The cold fit will not be felt at all, and the hot fit will be transformed into a sweat. On the intermittent day or days (when the fever is absent), take eight or ten grains of the Essence of Venus every morning and evening.

In acute, torturing, and tormenting pains, this is an admirable remedy. It restores the patient if the pain is not very deeply rooted; or, if it is rooted, it eases the intensity <note>original: horror</note>

<margin>
*Ens veneris* <note>original: "Essence of Venus"; a medicinal preparation typically made from copper or iron salts</note> or *Elementum ignis veneris* <note>original: "Element of Fire of Venus"</note>
</margin>

I may say confidently that I was the first person to make this in *England*—at least as far as was known in the year 1652. I prepared it for the Honorable *Robert Boyle, Esq.*, one of the members of the *Royal Society*, who has written of its excellence, as his published *Treatise* on the subject can testify. Of this medicine, *Helmont* <note>Jean Baptiste van Helmont (1580–1644), a Flemish chemist and physician highly influential in the 17th century</note> truly says that nothing works more powerfully upon the radical humidity <term>radical humidity: a vital moisture within the body thought to sustain life and health</term> than it does. 

It is most excellent for feminine diseases, especially menstrual blockages, rickets in children, lingering fevers, and obstructions of the spleen and mesaraics <term>mesaraics: the mesenteric veins or membranes of the abdomen</term>. It is especially helpful for women after childbirth, taking away after-pains to a remarkable degree and helping their "cleansings" <note>original: "cleansings"; refers to lochia, the normal discharge of blood and tissue after childbirth</note> so kindly and without violence that it is admirable. It also prevents all feverish symptoms to which many women are subject after labor <note>original: travel</note>, providing them incredible ease and comfort. 

However, I must assure those who are concerned that the batch I made in 1651 for the honorable *Robert Boyle, Esq.*—which he commended—is such an inferior preparation compared to the one I make now, that the former does not even deserve the same name as this latter version. This new version is as yellow as the purest gold and has a taste rich in the "Venus" <note>In alchemy, Venus represents copper; Starkey is referring to the metallic essence of the medicine</note>, so that it exceeds the other beyond all comparison and more closely approaches the "Element of Fire of Venus." I do not add this to disparage the former preparation, but to show the difference between that one and this.

This is the true remedy for sorrow <note>original: *Nepenthes verum*; from the Greek *nepenthes*, a legendary medicine used to banish grief</note> of the Philosophers, the true resinous medicine <note>original: *Ladanum*; a gummy resin used in pharmacy</note> without opium, lengthening the life, by God’s permission, and powerfully conquering monstrous and tragic ailments, such as epilepsy, stroke <note>original: *Apoplexy*</note>, palsy, coma, dizziness <note>original: *Vertigo*</note>, madness, hypochondriacal melancholy <term>hypochondriacal melancholy: a historical term for severe depression or anxiety, often thought to be caused by the digestive organs</term>, and uterine frenzies <note>original: *uterine Frensies*; historically used to describe intense psychological or physical distress in women</note>, along with several other similar diseases that stun the power of nature. In the Dutch edition <note>original: *Belgick Edition*</note> of Helmont’s works, under the title of the preparation of medicines, mention is made of a middle *Diaceltatesson* <term>Diaceltatesson: a specific alchemical compound medicine, typically involving minerals like antimony</term>, which is antimonial <term>antimonial: derived from the mineral antimony, used as a powerful purgative</term> and acts only as a purgative. I call this my "Common Diaceltatesson," and it is excellent for chronic diseases. It should be given every third day or every other day in a dose of 30 or 24 grains in two pills. On the alternative days, my sweating pill should be taken in the evening, with "Essence of Venus" <note>original: *Ens veneris*</note> in the morning. If the day is clear when the patient takes it, they may go out in the afternoon, provided they avoid excess <note>original: intemperancy</note>.

<meta>This page continues George Starkey’s description of advanced alchemical remedies, specifically focusing on the preparation and application of "Diaceltatesson"—a term used by the alchemists Paracelsus and van Helmont for a powerful chemical medicine—and other remedies for chronic and "hysterical" conditions.</meta>

<margin>
The true Diaceltatesson or Corallatum <note>original: *Corallatum*; a red, mercury-based chemical preparation</note> of Paracelsus and van Helmont.
</margin>
The other Diaceltatesson <term>Diaceltatesson: an alchemical term for a complex, transformative remedy, often based on mercury or copper, described by Paracelsus and van Helmont</term> is my true Corallatum. It radically cures gout, syphilis <note>original: *Lues*</note>, and all ulcers in the throat, lungs, bladder, and kidneys; it also roots out all fevers <note>original: *Agues*</note> and persistent fevers <note>original: *hectical fevers*</note>, no matter how long-standing they are. In cases where "the Pill" and the Essence of Venus <note>original: *Ens veneris*; a medicinal preparation of copper and sal ammoniac</note> fail, this remedy—given every day, or every other or third day, with a less powerful cleansing <note>original: *abstersive*</note> medicine in between—will cure the most desperate illnesses. It is a true secret remedy <note>original: *Arcanum*</note>, though it is not as highly excellent as the "Horizontal Gold," etc.

<margin>
Volatile alkali of Tartar, etc.
</margin>
There are also volatile spirits of alkalis, especially of tartar and tobacco. As van Helmont <note>Jan Baptiste van Helmont (1580–1644), a pioneering chemist and physician highly respected by Starkey</note> truly says, these cleanse all obstructions in the veins and remove the underlying causes of most diseases, especially if they are solidified <note>original: *coagulated*</note> upon dissolved solid substances <note>original: *Concrets*</note>. Regarding these, the praise <note>original: *Elogy*</note> given by Paracelsus and van Helmont is true: where this remedy reaches, nothing more powerful is needed. It cures through a universal cleansing <note>original: *abstersion*</note> and purification <note>original: *mundifying*</note> of the vessels, fulfilling the wishes of a careful physician to the great comfort of the patient. The dose is from 10 to 20 or 30 drops in white wine or ale while fasting.

<margin>
The essence of Amber, Salt of Steel, and volatile tincture of red coral.
</margin>
There is a great variety of hysterical disorders <note>original: *hifterical distempers*; historically, "hysteria" was a broad category of illnesses attributed to the uterus</note>. Physicians not unfittingly call the uterus in the female sex a "furious animal" <note>original: *animal furiosum*</note>, for in its extreme outbursts <note>original: *extravagant exorbitancies*</note>, it is strangely active in women. It causes all sorts of diseases—such as heart palpitations, dizziness <note>original: *vertigoes*</note>, epilepsy, and paralysis <note>original: *palsies*</note>, among other ailments—and is made worse by love, anger, grief, frights, and all intense passions. In these cases, "Salt of Steel" (specifically the "magisterial" salt of it, in which the entire substance of the steel/iron is transformed into a refined extract <note>original: *Magistery*</note> through a chemical <note>original: *Alcahestical*</note> preparation), as well as the Essence of Amber and the volatile tincture of red coral, are the appropriate specific remedies. These equal the "Great Secrets" <note>original: *grand Arcana*</note> of medicine. The dose of Salt of Steel is twenty grains.

<summary>This page details several potent alchemical remedies—including "Diaceltatesson," volatile alkalis, and "Salt of Steel"—used to treat chronic illnesses like gout, syphilis, and "hysterical" disorders attributed to the uterus.</summary>
<keywords>Diaceltatesson, Corallatum, Paracelsus, van Helmont, gout, syphilis, ague, alkali, tartar, salt of steel, hysteria, women's health, alchemical medicine</keywords>

<meta>This page continues George Starkey’s catalog of alchemical medicines. The previous page discussed the virtues of his "Diaceltatesson" (a complex mercury or copper remedy) and volatile alkalis for clearing obstructions in the body. This page concludes the description of his "Essence of Amber" before listing where his medicines can be purchased in London.</meta>

<margin>
* Note that this is *not* the Oil of Amber, but the whole Amber reduced to a magisterial essential salt, with only the "phlegm" <note>original: *Phlegme*; an alchemical term for a watery, inactive distillate</note> separated. This is produced without any burnt smell <note>original: *empyrheume*; the charred taste or odor resulting from overheating organic matter</note> by the "Great Circle" <note>original: *Circulatum majus*; a high-level alchemical solvent or continuous distillation process described by Helmont and Paracelsus</note> of van Helmont and Paracelsus.
</margin>

...of * Essence of Amber from 8 to 16 grains, with 10 drops of the volatile Tincture of Coral. These are also excellent remedies for scurvy <note>original: *Scorbutick*</note>, spleen issues <note>original: *Splenetick*</note>, and lung diseases <note>original: *pulmoniack*</note> in both men and women.

These, along with several other specific cleansing medicines <term>abstersives: substances used to wash away or purge morbid matter from the body</term>—such as spirit of salt <note>hydrochloric acid</note>, spirit of raw sulfur <note>original: *Sulphur vive*</note>, the essential sulfurous spirit of vitriol of Venus <note>copper sulfate</note>, antimony, and other substances, along with the concentrate <note>original: *Magistery*</note> and spirit of Saturn <note>lead</note>—are all truly prepared by George Starkey. As the need arises, they may be obtained from him with more detailed instructions for their use.

However, because my Pill and true Essence of Venus <note>original: *Ens veneris*; a medicinal preparation usually made from copper and sal ammoniac</note> are of such widespread and general use, I have decided to offer them for public sale. They may be purchased at the author’s house near the Exchange in Bartholomew Lane, by the Excise Office; at Mr. *Daforne's*, an accountant <note>original: *Accomptant*</note>; at Mr. *Samuel Watson’s* at the coffee house in *Ave-Mary Lane* on *Ludgate Hill*; at Mr. *Stone's* coffee house next door to the *Whalebone* in *Lothbury*; at Mr. *Livewell Chapman’s*, a bookseller <note>original: *Stationer*</note> in *Exchange Alley* near *Cornhill*; at the sign of *Morat the Great*, a coffee house in *Exchange Alley*; and at Mr. *Needler’s* on *Tower Wharf*.

## POSTSCRIPT.

Kind Reader, I ask for your fairness in judgment. Do not imagine that I am writing about theoretical concepts and mere ideas here, but rather what I have actually done, what I can do, and what I have the proper keys to accomplish. I shall be a master of the *Horizontal Gold* <note>a highly sought-after alchemical substance believed to be a universal medicine</note> before long, along with other secret remedies <note>original: *Arcana's*</note> such as the *Tincture of the Lily* <note>original: *Tinctura Lili*; a legendary alchemical remedy</note>, the *Male Metal* <note>original: *Metallus Masculus*</note>, its tincture reduced to a *Wine of Life* <note>original: *Vinum Vitæ*</note>, and the "milk" or "Element of Pearl." However, I must create these things one after another, as every true secret remedy <note>original: *Arcanum*</note> is genuinely tedious to prepare. Therefore, at present, I will only provide...

<summary>George Starkey concludes his descriptions of various alchemical preparations, providing a list of London locations where his medicines can be purchased, and asserts his expertise in creating high-level alchemical "Arcana."</summary>
<keywords>Essence of Amber, George Starkey, Ens veneris, Arcanum, alchemical medicine, London coffee houses, chemical pharmacy, van Helmont, Paracelsus</keywords>

--- Page 269 ---
<meta>This page continues George Starkey’s discussion of his medicinal preparations. He explains that while his primary "Pill" and "Ens Veneris" are available at specific shops, his more specialized "specific" remedies—and his rare "universal" medicines—must be obtained directly from him so he can provide expert guidance on their use.</meta>

...of myself, leaving the two previously mentioned medicines—my true pill and sincerely prepared *Ens Veneris* <note>original: *Ens veneris*; literally "Essence of Venus," usually a preparation of copper or iron vitriol</note>—at the places mentioned above. These are the most general in their virtues and are produced in the greatest quantities. 

Other "specifics"—targeted remedies that, in the cases for which they are designed, are equal to the *Arcana* <note>original: *Arcana*; powerful, secret alchemical remedies believed to have transformative healing properties</note>—include *Tincture of Coral* and the *Magisterial salt of Amber*. These are used for women’s illnesses and spleen issues in both sexes, as well as for scurvy <note>original: *scorbutick Cases*</note> and long-standing jaundice. There is also the milky liquid of *Crabs-eyes* <note>original: *Crab-eyes*; small, stony concentrations of calcium found in the stomachs of crayfish, historically used as an antacid and absorbent</note> for lingering, wasting fevers <note>original: *Tabid fevers*</note>, the after-pains of women in childbirth, and several other specific remedies. 

Because paper and time do not allow me to list these all by name, they shall be dispensed only by me. This is because there is a need for choice when several remedies apply to the same case. My experience with them enables me to better inform those who would use them, helping them make a better choice than they could on their own. Therefore, in cases where the pill and *Ens Veneris* either fail or promote the cure too slowly, I shall—upon receiving notice in writing—provide my advice and counsel in public writing, while concealing the names of both the patient and the doctor. 

In such emergencies, I will have the opportunity to advise, restrain, alter, or otherwise vary the administration of specific remedies, according to what I have learned through eighteen years of practice both "in the fire" <note>A reference to alchemical laboratory work involving furnaces</note> and in the art of medicine. Where the extremity of a disease cuts off all hope for specific remedies, I shall advise and supply true "universals" <note>In alchemy, a universal medicine or "panacea" was believed to cure all diseases by reinforcing the body's "vital light" or spirit</note> of the highest grade, but not otherwise. These are difficult and tedious to prepare, and **it is pointless to do with more what can be done with less** <note>original: *Frustra per plura fit, quod fieri potest per pauciora*; a philosophical principle often called Occam's Razor</note>. Scarcely one in forty diseases encountered in practice will require any remedy other than my pill, *Ens Veneris*, and *Diaceltatesson* <note>A complex chemical remedy often associated with mercury or copper preparations</note>. This last one will rarely be absolutely needed, but where it is, I am prepared to supply it to those who desire it.

<summary>George Starkey explains that while his general medicines are widely available, his more specialized and "universal" remedies require his personal consultation due to their potency and the expertise needed to select them. He emphasizes his eighteen years of alchemical experience and the sufficiency of his three primary medicines for most ailments.</summary>

*George Starkey*, known by the title of *Philosopher by the Fire* <note>original: *Philosopher by the fire*; a term for a practical chemist or alchemist who worked with furnaces to prepare medicines</note>.

<margin>An additional advertisement of very great importance.</margin>

Also available for "the stone" <note>original: *the stone*; kidney or bladder stones, which were a common and painful ailment in this period</note> is the *Ludus of Helmont* <note>original: *Ludus of Helmont*; a specific remedy described by the famous chemist Jan Baptista van Helmont, believed to dissolve stones</note> truly prepared by the same *George Starkey*. Anyone who has read *Helmont* is already aware of its virtues. The dose is *twenty drops*, which cures the condition completely <note>original: *radically*; meaning from the root</note>, both in the kidneys and the bladder, and removes all future tendency toward the illness.

## ADVERTISEMENT.

The method for making this *Pill* is accurately described by *Mr. Kendall* and others in the *Appendix* to the *Unlearned Alchemist* <note>original: *The Unlearned Alchymist*; a book by Starkey defending his chemical practices</note>, with the consent of *Mr. Starkey*, as you will find by his *Letter* on *page 43* of that book. That *Appendix*, along with the other book mentioned here called *Nature’s Explication* <note>original: *Natures Explication*; Starkey's 1657 work *Nature's Explication and Helmont's Vindication*</note>, may be obtained at the *Pelican* in *Little Britain*, *London*. *W. C. B.* <note>These initials likely refer to the publisher or bookseller; Little Britain was a street in London famous for its bookshops</note>.
